## 3. Spec questions

*Anything where the spec is ambiguous, silent, or seems wrong. Write it here and STOP rather than improvising — per CLAUDE.md. Move to "Resolved" once Chris answers; the answer should also get folded back into the spec itself as a changelog entry.*

### Open

*None currently.*

### Resolved

**Q10 — `playoff_start_week` ↔ `regular_season_weeks` consistency: §7.3.1 promises a validation §7.3.8 never defines, and no formula fits the printed ranges (filed by the M1 batch-6 remediation per R62, 2026-07-22; substance observed by L.A1.6/D60(12), previously carried only as ledger row F25 — this entry puts it in the ruling queue).**

**Evidence:** §7.3.1's `playoff_start_week` row (D 15 · R 14–17 · note *"Validated against `regular_season_weeks`."*) promises a cross-field check, but §7.3.8's validation-bullet list contains no such bullet and prints no formula — and no formula is consistent with the printed ranges: strict continuity `playoff_start_week = regular_season_weeks + 1` is **unsatisfiable at `regular_season_weeks = 12`** (would need start 13; the printed floor is 14) and makes start 17 unreachable (would need a 16-week regular season; the ceiling is 15), while anything looser permits **gap weeks** (e.g. regular 12 + start 17 → weeks 13–16 belong to neither the season nor the playoffs, and nothing defines what an idle league week IS — L.D0's schedule engine has no such concept). With no check at all — the shipped state — an **overlap** is expressible: `regular_season_weeks = 15` + `playoff_start_week = 14` parses clean and `validateLeagueSettings` accepts it (L.A1.6 implemented exactly the task-enumerated §7.3.8 bullets, deliberately not improvising a formula — D60(12)). The §7.3.8 arithmetic bullet (`start + rounds × weeks_per_round − 1 ≤ 18`) bounds the playoff END only; it says nothing about the seam.

**Options:**
- **(a) Strict continuity + range erratum (recommended).** Rule `playoff_start_week = regular_season_weeks + 1`; spec erratum adjusts the printed R from 14–17 to **13–16** (13 reachable only at a 12-week season) and the schema range follows. No overlap, no gap weeks, no new concepts; the field becomes fully derived — the wizard can render it read-only next to `regular_season_weeks` (the `playoff_byes = 'auto'` precedent), with the stored value kept for §12.1 compat. Incumbent-consistent: ESPN/Yahoo/Sleeper all derive the playoff start; none lets a commissioner express an overlap.
- **(b) No-overlap lower bound only.** Rule `playoff_start_week ≥ regular_season_weeks + 1`; printed ranges stay. Kills the overlap but leaves gap weeks expressible, and gap-week semantics (standings? waivers? locks?) would then need defining somewhere before M4's schedule engine — a bigger spec debt than the seam check itself.
- **(c) Explicit won't-validate ruling.** Record that §7.3.1's note is aspirational in v1; the per-field ranges are the only guard and seam consistency is the commissioner's problem. Cheapest, but ships a settings surface that accepts playoffs starting before the season ends — and the note column then needs an erratum anyway (it currently promises otherwise).

**Recommendation: (a).** It is the only option with neither silent overlap nor undefined gap-week semantics; the cost is a two-line erratum (§7.3.1 R column + note, one §7.3.8 bullet) plus a ~6-line check in `validateLeagueSettings` with boundary pins — one small PR once ruled.

**What's gated (precisely):** NOT the current lane heads — engine is at L.A1.10 and schema at L.A1.11, neither touches playoff-week validation, so **no §5 blocker is filed**. The ruling gates **L.A1.12**: `create_league` (+ the L.A2.1 wizard on top of it) is the first surface that can PERSIST an inconsistent pair — creation must not ship on the unruled validator. L.A1.13's settings PATCH is the second exposure, not the first (the F25 row's original routing said L.A1.13 — corrected per R62). L.A1.6 itself remains complete; this question is bookkeeping mis-filed at first, not a new halt.

**RULED 2026-07-22 (Chris): option (a) — verbatim answer "a" — strict continuity `playoff_start_week = regular_season_weeks + 1`.** Applied 2026-07-25 (branch `feat/M1-Q10-playoff-seam-ruling` — the "one small PR once ruled" this entry promised): spec erratum **v2.8.6** (§7.3.1 R column 14–17 → **13–16**, note now names the formula; ONE new §7.3.8 seam bullet — no overlap, no gap weeks; changelog carries the rationale: 13 reachable only at a 12-week season, 17 unreachable and dropped, incumbent-consistent, field effectively derived). Implementation: seam check in `validateLeagueSettings` with a per-field message + the 13–16 schema range in `league-settings.ts`; the old live-probe overlap pair (regular 15 + start 14) is now the pinned REGRESSION TRAP (exact message, sole error), all four boundary edges (12→13/13→14/14→15 defaults/15→16) pinned passing, one-past pinned failing both directions, and the check applies even at `playoff_teams = 0` — the field is derived, not conditional (D67(1); the D60(5) points-only skip covers only the playoff-END arithmetic bullet). Defaults (14+15) and the round-trip fixture (13+14) already satisfied (a) — pinned, no value change needed. The wizard's read-only-derived rendering (the `playoff_byes = 'auto'` precedent, stored value kept for §12.1 compat) is L.A2.1's build concern — ledgered as **F27**. B4 cleared; F25 flipped ✅; D67. **L.A1.12 unblocked.**

**Q9 — ESPN's verified in-product default kicking table (60+ FG tier) — and two D/ST micro-categories — cannot be expressed by the App B.4 catalog: ESPN template parity gap (filed by L.A1.9, 2026-07-21; STOP-CONDITION HALT per the task's re-verify rule — "Divergences from App B: stop and file a spec question").**

L.A1.9's build-time re-verification (task text item 1) ran the full sanctioned source pass on 2026-07-21 via the in-app browser (the L.A1.7 working path). **The F19 obligations themselves both RESOLVED cleanly** — evidence below, recorded here so no session refetches it — but the same pass surfaced a **new** App B divergence that the ESPN templates cannot express, and the task text is explicit that a divergence is a stop. No migration was written; nothing was seeded.

**What was retrieved (all 2026-07-21, in-app browser):**

1. **ESPN Scoring-Formats article** (support.espn.com/hc/en-us/articles/360003914032-Scoring-Formats) — re-fetched; standard PA table matches v2.8.3's transcription value-for-value (0/1–6/7–13/14–17/18–27/28–34/35–45/46+ at +5/+4/+3/+1/0/−1/−3/−5); the R50 finer custom-options PA list (2–6/18–21/22–27) still present, still values-free; nine YA buckets in custom options, **no standard YA values** (the F19(a) gap, reconfirmed). Standard offense: pass 0.04/TD 4/INT −2/2pt 2 · rush 0.1/6/2 · rec 0.1/6/2 (PPR +1/rec) · fumble lost −2 · KR/PR/fumble-recovery TD 6. **Standard kicking (the new finding): FG 0–39 = 3 · 40–49 = 4 · 50–59 = 5 · 60+ = 6 · PAT 1 · FG missed (any) = −1; no PAT-missed penalty.**
2. **ESPN D/ST article** (support.espn.com/hc/en-us/articles/115003847231) — re-fetched; split model reconfirmed ("D/ST begins each game with 10 points… given up 0 points (worth 5 points)… allowed 0 offensive yards (worth 5 points)") → the `def_ya_0_99 = +5` anchor. Also publishes two PAT micro-rules: **D/ST safety during a PAT = +1; D/ST returns the ball to its own end zone during a PAT = +2** (see finding 2 below).
3. **ESPN in-product defaults — the F19 pull (the product's own settings source, no auth):** `lm-api-reads.fantasy.espn.com/apis/v3/games/ffl/seasons/2026/segments/0/leaguedefaults/1?view=mSettings` (Standard) and `…/leaguedefaults/3?view=mSettings` (PPR).    - Standard (`leaguedefaults/1`): `[20,−2] [72,−2] [85,−1] [89,0,{16:5}] [90,0,{16:4}] [91,0,{16:3}] [92,0,{16:1}] [95,0,{16:2}] [96,0,{16:2}] [97,0,{16:2}] [98,0,{16:2}] [99,0,{16:1}] [123,0,{16:−1}] [124,0,{16:−3}] [125,0,{16:−5}] [128,0,{16:5}] [129,0,{16:3}] [130,0,{16:2}] [132,0,{16:−1}] [133,0,{16:−3}] [134,0,{16:−5}] [135,0,{16:−6}] [136,0,{16:−7}] [3,0.04] [24,0.1] [42,0.1] [86,1] [209,1,{16:1}] [19,2] [26,2] [44,2] [206,2,{16:2}] [80,3] [4,4] [77,4] [198,5] [25,6] [43,6] [63,6] [93,6,{16:6}] [101,6,{16:6}] [102,6,{16:6}] [103,6,{16:6}] [104,6,{16:6}] [201,6]`
   - PPR (`leaguedefaults/3`): identical **plus `[53,1]`** (receptions = 1) — nothing else differs.
   - Interpretation method (recorded for auditability — the payload is id-coded): the value multiset aligns one-for-one with the Scoring-Formats article's standard tables (offense 0.04/4/−2/2 · 0.1/6/2 · 0.1/6/2 · −2; PA +5/+4/+3/+1/−1/−3/−5 in the contiguous id runs 89–92 and 123–125; D/ST 2/2/2/2/1 + four 6-pt return TDs; kicking 3/4/5/6/1/−1), with **zero-valued buckets omitted from the payload** — PA 18–27 and YA 300–349 are exactly the article's 0-point rows and exactly the ids missing from the otherwise-contiguous runs. Two independent anchors pin the YA read: the head anchor `def_ya_0_99 = +5` (D/ST article) and the omitted-zero convention proven on the PA side.
   - **F19(a) RESOLVED — ESPN standard YA values (ids 128–136 in published-bucket order):** `<100 = +5 · 100–199 = +3 · 200–299 = +2 · 300–349 = 0 (omitted) · 350–399 = −1 · 400–449 = −3 · 450–499 = −5 · 500–549 = −6 · 550+ = −7`. Anchor matches. Unambiguous.
   - **F19(b) RESOLVED — the product's default model renders the STANDARD bucketization:** the default payload contains the standard list's ids (PA 1–6 present at +4; 18–27 absent-as-zero) and none of the finer custom-options buckets (no 2–6/18–21/22–27 items). The finer list is the LM custom bucket menu only; it is score-invisible at standard values as R50 recorded. The seeded def_pa keys stand; no additional keys needed; the templates (when they land) relate to the finer list as: not rendered by the default product, representable only in the v1.1 custom unlock (where F21's family-grouping applies).
4. **Yahoo SLN6489** (help.yahoo.com/kb/SLN6489.html) — full defaults retrieved (accordion DOM extraction): offense 0.04 (25 yds/pt) · pass TD 4 · **INT −1** · 0.1 rush/rec · TDs 6 · return TD 6 · off-fum-ret TD 6 · one combined 2-pt category = 2 · fumble lost −2 · **receptions 0.5 (the platform default = Half PPR, as §7.3.3 says)**; kicking FG 0–19/20–29/30–39 = 3 · 40–49 = 4 · 50+ = 5 · PAT 1 (**no miss penalties in defaults**; the sub-40 finer buckets are score-equal to `fg_0_39 = 3`); D/ST sack 1 · INT 2 · FR 2 · TD 6 · safety 2 · blocked kick 2 · KR/PR TD 6 · PA exactly the shared family: 0 = 10 · 1–6 = 7 · 7–13 = 4 · 14–20 = 1 · 21–27 = 0 · 28–34 = −1 · 35+ = −4 (single model, no YA — per spec). **Every Yahoo cell expressible in catalog keys; App B.1 Yahoo cells all reconfirmed.**
5. **Sleeper scoring-options article** (support.sleeper.com/en/articles/3998131-scoring-settings) — categories reconfirmed (PA = shared family buckets; YA buckets = ESPN's nine; FG buckets 0–19/20–29/30–39/40–49/50+, **no 60+ tier**) — but the article publishes **no default values**, and a support-center search ("default league settings") finds no Sleeper defaults article. Sleeper's in-product creation defaults sit behind account login (out of bounds). **Per the house verification rule (this task's charter): Sleeper's six template value-sets ship as App B best-known values FLAGGED unverified (ledger row on landing), never silently trusted.**

**The blocking finding (1): ESPN's default kicking table has a 60+ = 6 tier — App B.4's three-bucket shape (`fg_0_39`/`fg_40_49`/`fg_50_plus` at 3/4/5) cannot express it.** Product-confirmed twice, independently: the Scoring-Formats standard table (60+ = 6, 50–59 = 5) and both in-product default payloads (`[198,5]` = FG 50–59, `[201,6]` = FG 60+ — 198/201 are the only unaccounted 5/6-valued kicker ids, and their values match the article exactly). This is not a value drift on an existing key — it is a **category the engine's namespace cannot represent**: no `fg_50_59`/`fg_60_plus` keys exist, no columns exist, and — decisive — **the Sleeper feed has no 60+ signal** (`fgm_50p` aggregates everything ≥50; the L.A1.7 adapter audit lists sub-40 buckets but nothing above 50), so even new keys would have no data source on the current tier. Consequence: cent parity for ESPN templates is unachievable as-is on made 60+ FGs (order ~5–15 league-wide per NFL season; −1 pt each vs ESPN's product).

**The blocking finding (2), same class, smaller:** ESPN's defaults score two D/ST PAT micro-categories — PAT-safety = +1 (`[209,1,{16:1}]`) and PAT-return = +2 (`[206,2,{16:2}]`), both also published in the D/ST article. No catalog keys, no columns, no feed signal on any current provider tier (order ~1–3 plays league-wide per season).

**Why this is a stop and not an L.A1.7-style recorded erratum:** L.A1.7's charter explicitly licensed "seed what ESPN actually publishes and note the delta" (additive derived keys, no storage or feed dependency). L.A1.9's charter says the opposite — "Divergences from App B: stop and file a spec question (parity guarantee is the product promise — never silently ship drifted values)" — and this divergence has real options with product impact (below), including one that would add player_stats columns (schema beyond the task). Value-level per-platform differences (Yahoo's no-miss-penalty kicking, ESPN's no-PAT-miss) are NOT treated as divergences: B.1 defers K/D-ST to "each platform's own tables (⚠ verify)", so the verified platform table governs those cells; the stop is specifically for categories the catalog/namespace cannot express.

**Options:**
- **(a) Documented parity exception (recommended).** ESPN templates ship `fg_50_plus = 5`; the 60+ extra point and the two PAT micro-categories become a **named, documented parity exception** in §7.3.3/App B (spec changelog entry): "ESPN parity is cent-exact except made 60+ FGs (−1 pt vs ESPN) and D/ST PAT-safety/PAT-return (unscored) — categories the current data tier cannot observe." Rationale: the alternative keys would be **permanently pending/zero** for lack of any feed signal — a standing E61 state manufactured on every ESPN league every week is strictly worse than a documented ~1-pt/season-scale exception; and the parity guarantee's operative promise ("a migrating league's scores feel identical") survives a documented exception, not a standing honest-badge.
- **(b) Add `fg_50_59`/`fg_60_plus` (+ PAT-micro) keys and columns now.** Expresses the table, but no provider signal exists to fill them — every ESPN league shows permanent pending on those keys (E61), and it adds player_stats columns from inside a task chartered for `is_template` + seed only.
- **(c) = (a) + a September F-row:** the F10 raw-endpoint inspection window additionally checks whether Sleeper's raw feed carries a 60+ FG spelling (e.g. `fgm_60p`) and PAT-play detail; if a signal exists, upgrading ESPN kicking parity becomes the one-PR §23.5 data task it should be, and the exception shrinks or closes.
- **Recommendation: (a) + (c)'s September check.** One ruling line unblocks L.A1.9; the post-ruling build is mechanical (all six platforms' evidence is already in this entry).

**What's blocked:** L.A1.9 (migration 058 + pgTAP 012 + the 6 template rows) and the schema lane behind it (L.A1.11), plus L.A1.10/L.A1.12's template-row dependencies. **Not blocked:** L.A1.6 (engine lane, pure TS — still open and unstarted). F19 stays ⬜ Open (it discharges only when the ESPN rows land) but both its sub-obligations are answered above; the landing session should cite this entry in the 058 banner rather than refetching.

**RULED 2026-07-22 (Chris): option (a) + (c).** Ship `fg_50_plus = 5`; the ESPN 60+ FG tier (6 pts) and the two PAT micro-categories (+1 PAT-safety, +2 PAT-return) are **NAMED PARITY EXCEPTIONS**, not pending categories — recorded as a spec erratum (**v2.8.5**: §7.3.3 parity-guarantee erratum + App B.4 annotation + changelog) with the rationale (provider-unobservable, ~5–15 plays league-wide per season, permanent-pending would violate E61 semantics). **Condition:** the exceptions must be user-visible in the ESPN templates' description/detail metadata — a plain-language line a commissioner can read — not only in migration banners. **(c):** the September F10 check additionally looks for a Sleeper raw-feed 60+ spelling (e.g. `fgm_60p`) and PAT-play detail; if a signal exists, the tier becomes expressible as a one-PR §23.5 data task and the exception lifts — path noted in the erratum. Implementation: migration **058** + pgTAP **012** + `src/lib/leagues/scoring/templates.ts` (D59); the exception line lives in `scoring_systems.description` (the schema's existing detail column — a natural home, no schema change needed) and is pinned verbatim by pgTAP 012 and the templates tests. Sleeper's unpublished default values ship App-B-flagged per the house rule — ledger **F24**. B3 cleared; F19 flipped ✅.

**Q8 — client-writable league state: the spec-kept owner FOR ALL policy vs server-authoritative (§8.1) (filed by the M1 batch-2 review, R37/R38, 2026-07-20).** The review live-proved an ordinary authenticated user forging `scoring_rules_snapshot` + `status='drafting'` (and able to hard-DELETE a league, cascading `league_members`/`team_managers`) through 001's "League owners can manage" FOR ALL policy — which spec §12.1 explicitly said to keep — and a commissioner client-reseating `league_members.team_id` with no `team_managers` stint (cache⇄history divergence) through §12.2's printed commish FOR ALL policy.
**RULED 2026-07-20 (Chris): once created, league state is server-authoritative — no direct client DML.** Replace 001's "League owners can manage" FOR ALL with owner SELECT only (league creation already happens via RPC per the M1 task specs; the §15.1 deletion flow is a commish soft delete via RPC, so no client DELETE verb is retained — verified against the spec before implementing). Remove client UPDATE on `league_members` seating fields — reseating happens only via the L.A1.12–15 RPCs. The spec's "keep the owner policy" instruction is **superseded**: it predates 040's protected columns (`scoring_rules_snapshot`, `status`, `settings`), which turned a benign legacy policy into a forgery surface. Recorded in spec **v2.8.2**; implemented as migration **054** (D52).

**Q7 — Q4-application conflicts the conflict report didn't surface (filed 2026-07-20; STOP-CONDITION HALT of L.A1.1).** Recording the Q4 ruling (usernames 5–20, permanent) against the actual codebase hit two conflicts that C5 never surfaced, tripping the Builder session's ruling-vs-codebase stop condition — the migration was **not** written:

1. **AI persona accounts live in `profiles.username` with hyphenated handles** — `fieldscout-ai` (system owner) plus the ~19-persona roster (`bathew-merry-ai`, `zj-jachariason-ai`, …; `src/lib/personas/roster.ts`), created as real auth users + profiles rows by `scripts/seed-ai-personas.ts:88`, **existing in production** (the personas feature is live). *[Corrected — see D49(9)/R36: the roster personas are `ai_personas` rows, not profiles rows; only `fieldscout-ai` holds a profiles row. The conflict (and the ruling below) stands either way — the exempt pattern must cover every persona-shape handle a privileged writer seeds.]* A blocklist test *enforces* the `-ai` suffix (`blocklist.test.ts:41`), and the handles key avatar file paths + public URLs. Every one violates the `[a-z0-9_]` charset §7.2 gives the username contract — the CHECK as ruled would **fail the prod push** and break persona seeding. Needs your call: **(a)** exempt persona/system accounts — the CHECK becomes `human-pattern OR persona-pattern` (e.g. hyphenated, `-ai`-suffixed). Bonus: keeping hyphens out of the human charset means **no human can ever register a `*-ai` handle** — free namespace separation for the AI-transparency convention; **(b)** allow hyphens for everyone (loses that separation); **(c)** rename personas to underscores (touches public URLs, avatar files on disk, the blocklist tests, and any indexed content). **Recommendation: (a).**
2. **The account-settings page ships a live username-**change** flow** (`src/app/app/settings/page.tsx:173–181` — editable field, saves `username` with its own 3–30 validator), contradicting PERMANENT. Related definitional wrinkle: "no changing after creation" needs a boundary — signup *creates* the profile with an auto-generated `user_xxxxxxxx` placeholder, and the username-selection page (`/username`) is itself an UPDATE, so a literal no-update-after-creation guard would break onboarding. **Recommendation:** permanent = **after explicit selection** (the placeholder is pre-selection); remove the username field from settings (display-only `@handle`); typo/regret recovery is a support/admin path, not self-serve; a DB-level permanence guard is deferred until the selection flow moves server-side (an RPC that can tell selection from rename).
3. *(Minor, listed for completeness:)* `scripts/seed-dev-user.ts` seeds username `'dev'` (3 chars) — breaks under the 5-minimum; mechanical rename (e.g. `dev_user`/`dev_pro`) ships with the Q4 migration.

**What's blocked:** L.A1.1 (migration 040 carries the Q4 constraint per your ruling) and the serialized schema lane behind it. **Not blocked:** L.A1.6 (engine lane, pure TS). The Q5/Q6 rulings had no conflicts and are fully recorded/applied. One line from you on (1) and (2) unblocks the lane.
**RESOLVED 2026-07-20 (Chris): recommendations accepted for items 1, 2, and 3.** (1) Persona exemption **(a)**: the DB CHECK is `username ~ '^[a-z0-9_]{5,20}$' OR username ~ '^[a-z0-9]+(-[a-z0-9]+)*-ai$'` — human charset stays hyphen-free, so no human can register a `*-ai` handle (enforced at the DB layer by a 025-pattern guard trigger blocking persona-pattern usernames for `authenticated`/`anon`, since the CHECK alone can't tell a persona insert from a hostile PostgREST rename). (2) Permanent = **after explicit selection** — the signup-time `user_xxxxxxxx` placeholder is pre-selection and the `/username` page's UPDATE is the sanctioned selection write; the settings-page username field becomes **display-only**; typo/regret recovery is a support/admin path; a DB-level permanence guard is deferred until the selection flow moves server-side (documented residual: a determined user can still rename to a *valid human* name via direct PostgREST until then). (3) `seed-dev-user.ts` username `'dev'` → `'dev_user'`. Implementation: L.A1.1/040 (D49). B2 cleared.

**Q4 — username identity contract: §7.2 vs deployed reality vs spec-auth-profiles (filed per C5, 2026-07-20).** §7.2 requires `username` be **citext, 3–20 chars `[a-z0-9_]`**, and treats usernames as **changeable** ("league surfaces update live"; E52 tests a mid-season rename). Deployed reality: plain `TEXT UNIQUE NOT NULL`, zero DB constraints; the only validation is a client-side regex allowing **3–30 chars, must start with a letter** (lowercased app-side at write); and `spec-auth-profiles.md` says usernames are **permanent in V1**. Three product calls needed: (a) do existing 21–30-char handles stay valid (grandfather) or get migrated? (b) citext conversion vs a `lower(username)` unique index? (c) changeable (per §7.2) or permanent (per auth spec) for v1? **Recommendation:** grandfather existing handles; enforce §7.2's charset + 3–20 length on *new/changed* usernames only (DB CHECK scoped via trigger on change); take the `lower(username)` unique index instead of a citext type migration (same case-insensitive semantics, no column rewrite); adopt §7.2's *changeable* contract — league surfaces join on `user_id` so renames are already safe, and the auth spec's "permanent" reads as a V1 simplification this epic supersedes (fold into both specs' changelogs on resolution). **M1 interim (D36, not gated on this):** invite-by-username matches via `lower(username)`; no column migration ships until you rule.
**RULED 2026-07-20 (Chris): usernames are minimum 5 characters, maximum 20, and PERMANENT (no changing after creation).** Supersedes both §7.2's 3–20/changeable contract and the deployed 3–30 client rule. No production users → no grandfathering: the DB constraint itself moves to 5–20 as part of the schema work (Builder's call on home: **folded into 040** — noted in tasks-M1 L.A1.1). Uniqueness stays case-insensitive via a `lower(username)` unique index (no citext conversion). Recorded in spec **v2.8** (§7.2 table + prose; E52 obsoleted). **Application halted same-session on Q7** (persona `*-ai` handles + the settings-page rename flow — neither surfaced by the conflict report); the ruling itself stands.

**Q5 — transactional email vendor for seat-targeted invites (filed per C6, 2026-07-20).** §7.2 makes email the **primary** invite channel ("a real email is sent with a claim link") — and the repo has **zero** outbound-email capability (no email lib, no edge functions, production SMTP commented out; only Supabase Auth's built-in mails + the local dev capture inbox). Needed from you: vendor choice, API key, and a `fieldscout.gg` sender domain (DNS records). **Recommendation:** Resend (Next.js-native, template-friendly, free tier covers beta). **M1 interim (D37, not gated on this):** the invite flow ships complete behind an `EmailSender` seam with a dev/log implementation (local capture inbox in dev), so flipping the real vendor on is config + a ~20-line adapter whenever the account exists.
**RULED 2026-07-20 (Chris): no email vendor is chosen now.** M1 builds invite email-sending behind a seam — **interface only, no vendor binding** (D37 confirmed as recommended). The v1 minimum bar, which must work regardless of vendor: **the league join link is always visible and copyable by the league manager**, so they can paste it into any email/text themselves (tasks-M1 L.A2.5 updated). Vendor selection deferred until the invite-send feature is ready to ship mail. Recorded in spec **v2.8**.

**Q6 — three doc edits only you should approve (filed per C1/C2/C13, 2026-07-20).** (a) **CLAUDE.md Key Business Rule #5** says "Leagues are Pro only. Gate league creation **and joining**" — the spec says joining is free (×3); any session following rule 5 literally would Pro-gate the join route and break §7.2. Edit to: "League **creation** is Pro only; joining is free." (b) **CLAUDE.md Redesign section** still says league/team/live-draft screens are "UI-only… do not invent API routes or schema" — stale since the epic was greenlit (M0 shipped schema; M1 ships routes). Scope it to non-greenlit surfaces or delete. (c) **spec-leagues-live-mode.md** contradicts the epic (join Pro-only, `commissioner_id`, thin schema) and §2.3 formally supersedes it, but the file carries no banner saying so — add one pointing at spec-redraft-leagues.md. **Recommendation:** all three are one-line edits; say the word and the next session applies them verbatim. M1 builds to the spec meanwhile.
**RULED 2026-07-20 (Chris), item (a) — and wider: NEITHER creating NOR joining a league requires Pro.** Supersedes §7.2's "Create league (Pro only)" and CLAUDE.md Business Rule #5, **rewritten this session** to: "League creation and joining are free. Pro-level league features come later." Spec swept in **v2.8** (§3.1 goal 6, §7.2, §15.1, §16 preamble, §17 matrix, §19.1, §20 metric retired, Appendix C L.A1); tasks-M1 de-Pro'd — **DoD changes flagged in-task**: L.A1.12 (requireProUser gate + in-body is_pro check + the non-Pro 402 `PRO_REQUIRED` test all deleted; replaced by "a free user creates successfully") and L.A2.1 (Pro upsell/402 state removed from the wizard). Items (b) CLAUDE.md redesign bullet and (c) spec-leagues-live-mode superseded banner were NOT ruled and stay open under the tasks-doc conflict report (C2/C13).

**Q3 — split D/ST model (`dst_model = 'split'`, ESPN): no canonical core_box stat keys (filed per R8, 2026-07-19).** The ESPN parity templates — 2 of the 6 v1 templates — score D/ST as "split +5 pts / +5 yds (start +10)" (spec App B comparison table; `dst_model = 'split'` per the App B kicking/D-ST note), which needs points-allowed *and yards-allowed* tier-indicator keys. The registry has zero yards-allowed keys and the spec names no canonical split-model keys (B.4 names only the single-model `def_pa_*` family). D21 covered middle-tier naming for the single model only; the Q2/v2.7 punt covers B.2–B.3, not this core_box gap. Not an M0 blocker, but M1's ESPN template-parity work lands on it. **Recommendation:** extend the D21 convention — keep `def_pa_<lo>_<hi>` as the shared points side and add a `def_ya_<lo>_<hi>` yards-allowed family on ESPN's own bucket boundaries (confirm the exact buckets from ESPN's published table when building the M1 parity fixtures); registry is additive, so the keys land with M1's template work once blessed. Needs Chris/Architect sign-off on the naming before M1 seeds them.
**RESOLVED 2026-07-19 (Chris): approved as recommended.** The ESPN split-D/ST model gets registry keys named `def_ya_<lo>_<hi>`, per the D21 convention and Appendix B.4; `def_pa_<lo>_<hi>` stays the shared points side. Exact ESPN bucket boundaries confirmed from ESPN's published table when M1 builds the parity fixtures. Seeding the actual keys belongs to whichever task next touches the stat-key registry (registry is additive) — resolution recorded here, no keys seeded with it. Fold into the spec changelog when the keys land.

**Q1 — `sleeper_free` can't meet the live-provider hard requirements (kickoff timestamps + official inactives).** Spec §23.1 makes official inactives ~90 min pre-kickoff and in-game injury designations a hard requirement of whichever *live* provider runs, and §23.3 derives every lock from `nfl_games.kickoff_at` at evaluation time — but the free Sleeper schedule endpoint is day-granularity only, there is no official-inactives feed in the current integration, and nothing writes `nfl_games`.
**RESOLVED 2026-07-18 (Chris): proceed with nflverse.** `sleeper_free` stays the stats source; nflverse supplements kickoff timestamps (and is the likely official-inactives source). Engineering notes (D16): the nflverse adapter lands with the first runtime consumer of kickoffs (locks/schedule milestone — not M0); nflverse schedule/inactives data is retroactively fetchable, so the 2026 fixture library can be back-filled at any time — accepted limitation: back-fill loses real *arrival timing* of the ~90-min-pre-kickoff inactives publication (synthetic scenarios cover that timing per §23.1). M0's recorder proceeds as specced.

**Q2 — canonical advanced stat-key names: §23.5 vs Appendix B conflict.** §7.3.3's dot-product requires one shared key namespace, but §23.5 says `air_yards`/`yards_after_catch` while Appendix B.2 scores `receiving_air_yards`/`receiving_yac`.
**RESOLVED 2026-07-18 (Chris): punted — the named advanced stats were examples, not v1 commitments.** Misunderstanding corrected: air yards / YAC / yards-after-contact in §23.5/Appendix B are **illustrative** of the long-run advanced-scoring direction, contingent on raising money and funding a paid real-time stats API (or building one). No canonical naming decision is needed now. Consequences: M0 seeds `STAT_KEYS` with `core_box` only; the `tracking`/`charted` **tier machinery** (capability tiers, two-phase settle, revision handling — the §7.3.3 extensibility contract, which *is* v1-committed) is proven with clearly-marked placeholder keys (D15); real keys become a one-PR data task when funded (§23.5 checklist). Recorded in spec changelog **v2.7**; M1's Alpha/Ultra backtest exit criterion deferred (delivery plan **v1.3**). **Follow-up resolved same day (Chris): the v1 template picker ships the 6 parity templates only** — no Alpha/Ultra teaser cards; the FieldScout cards and the "same game, scored three ways" widget return when advanced stats are funded (spec §7.3.3/§16/App B/App C annotated).

## 4. Decisions log

*Build-time engineering decisions made during implementation that aren't spelled out verbatim in the spec — naming, library choices, minor sequencing calls. Product/scope decisions belong in the spec's own changelog (see spec-redraft-leagues.md), not here; this log is for "how we built it," not "what we're building."*

**2026-07-18 — Architect (M0).** Full rationale in `docs/specs/tasks-M0-foundations.md` §3:
- **D1** Engine home `src/lib/leagues/` — pure TS (no Next/Node APIs, no `process.env`, no `fetch` outside adapters) so it ports to Deno Edge Functions later.
- **D2** `TimeProvider` = `{ now(): Date }`; `VirtualClock` has step mode + wall-paced 1×/4×/64×.
- **D3** ESLint ban on `Date.now()`/argless `new Date()` in `src/lib/leagues/**` + `src/lib/sync/live-stats.ts` (mechanical, not convention). *Annotations 2026-07-19: (R17) the shipped override covers `src/lib/leagues/**` only — live-stats.ts still legitimately reads wall time today (the D12 stamp); the guard extends to it with the L.A0.2b seam refactor. (R15) selector set widened beyond the original two forms (bare `Date()`, `globalThis.Date`/`globalThis.performance`, computed `Date['now']`, `performance.now()`, Date aliasing, spread-into-`new Date`); still lint-level, so exotic indirection (e.g. `Reflect.get`) remains uncatchable — the guard is a tripwire, not a proof. (R17 deferral closed 2026-07-19: L.A0.2b extended the override to live-stats.ts, deliberate-violation probe shown.)*
- **D4** Spec's "`getInjuries/Inactives`" (§23.1) = two methods, `getInjuries` + `getInactives` (different shapes/cadences).
- **D5** One canonical stat-key namespace seeded from Appendix B.1–B.5 (names pending Q2); `storage: 'column'|'advanced'|'deferred'`; keys without columns not persisted in M0; capability *gating* starts M1.
- **D6** Fixture format: JSONL.gz in-repo, `{t, method, args, ok, status, body}`, replay = latest response ≤ virtual now.
- **D7** Pre-September gate data: synthetic multi-poll recording is the determinism fixture; a recorded real 2025 week is the cross-check; gate re-runs on the first real 2026 week.
- **D8** No `player_stats.advanced` migration in M0 (first persisting milestone adds it).
- **D9** pgTAP + local-stack bootstrap pulled into M0 (L.A0.5a) — DoD §2.3 requires it and the repo has none.
- **D10** `nfl_weeks` broadcast-trigger waiver: plan §8.1's realtime line waived (no subscribers; seed-only service-role writes); trigger ships with the milestone that live-updates `first_kickoff_at`/`last_game_ends_at`.
- **D11** 1×/4×/64× gate reading: full week step-driven ×3 identical + wall-paced ≥3-poll-interval slice equivalence (a full week at 1× is ~5 days — infeasible).
- **D12** Refactored ingestion stamps `updated_at` from injected time (deliberate payload change; wall-clock stamp at live-stats.ts:114 would break replay determinism by construction).
- **D13** Spec erratum v2.6.1: `nfl_games.kickoff` → `kickoff_at` (5 refs; matches deployed 001 schema).
- **D14** `source: 'sleeper'` preserved exactly through the seam refactor (002's `'live'` comment is stale; downstream branches on in-use values).

**2026-07-19 — Builder (L.A0.3):**
- **D26** Synthetic-tier design points. (1) **PRNG streams are keyed by (seed, playerId) only — never scenario id** (FNV-1a + mulberry32, `synthetic/prng.ts`): same-seed scenarios share stat lines, which is what makes the `provider_outage` back-fill proof an *equality against a same-seed `happy_path` world* instead of a self-referential recomputation. (2) **Skeleton/values split:** the timing/event skeleton (slate, kickoffs, event instants, player pool, inactives) is a fixed function of scenario id; only stat values draw from the seed — asserted by the "different seed → different lines, same skeleton" transcript test. (3) Stat lines are `floor(finalLine × progress)` — in-game deltas monotone by construction, outage recovery is cumulative back-fill for free (§23.2). (4) The synthetic tier supplies **real `kickoffAt` timestamps** (plus `gameDate` derived from them) — it's the tier that *can* (§23.6); flex/postponement reveal their new kickoff only from `announceAt`. (5) `Math.random` banned under `src/lib/leagues/**` via lint selectors. *(R22 erratum, corrected 2026-07-19: as originally shipped this was only two selectors — call + computed-property forms — strictly narrower than the D3 guard this entry called it, leaving globalThis routing, Math aliasing, and `crypto.getRandomValues` lint-clean. Widened to full R15 parity on `fix/review-findings-batch2-2026-07-19` (`bf45948`): globalThis.Math, Math alias/destructure (declarator + assignment), and crypto.getRandomValues (direct/computed/globalThis-routed) all banned; seven deliberate-violation probes shown failing. "D3-style" is accurate as of that commit.)* (6) Charted placeholder values are **absent until `chartedPostAt`, never 0** (§23.5) — a deliberate-break probe (emit 0 pre-post) was shown failing 2 tests, then reverted. (7) Base week is 2026 wk2, matching the seeded `nfl_weeks` calendar (039), so L.A0.6's calendar assertion (gate item 2c) composes without remapping. Library versioning: `SCENARIO_LIBRARY_VERSION` + per-scenario `version`, both 1. *(Both bumped to 2 on 2026-07-19: R25 changed `gameDate` from the UTC day to the ET calendar day — the first exercise of the bump-on-behavior-change contract, now enforced mechanically by the R23 golden pins.)*

**2026-07-19 — Builder (L.A0.4):**
- **D27** Fixture identity header. D6's format ("one line per provider call") gains a line-1 metadata header `{format: 'fieldscout-fixture', version: 1, provider, season, week}` — additive, versioned, and parse-rejected loudly on mismatch. Rationale: the D24 self-review note — fixtures carry source semantics downstream branching doesn't know; the recorded provider's identity must live IN the artifact, not be guessed from its path. `RecordingStatsProvider` stays a transparent wrapper (exposes the inner provider's `name`/`capabilities`; the header is written by the harness/CLI that owns the file).
- **D28** Replay identity: `FixtureReplayProvider.name = 'fixture:<recorded provider>'` (the §4 sketch's `fixture:<id>` form) — honest about data origin, so a replay ingesting into any real table writes a `source` value that says what it is. The recorded identity is exposed as `recordedProviderName` for the consumer that deliberately wants byte-identical live-session rows (L.A0.6's call when it composes the gate); the replayer never impersonates its source silently. Replay `capabilities` defaults to core_box, overridable at construction (a fixture is its source's tier; gating starts M1/D5). Also: Date revival on replay is a strict-shape reviver (full `…T…Z` ISO timestamps only — `gameDate`'s day-only strings never revive), and both before-first-recorded-response and never-recorded calls throw descriptively rather than inventing data (§23.2 "never wrong numbers"). Single-poll fixtures: the five method stamps span the poll's real wall duration and the D6 anchor maps to the FIRST stamp — replay harnesses step past the poll before reading (pinned in the cross-check test).
- **D23** Explicit grants strategy (resolves D18): migration `037_explicit_api_grants.sql` versions production's grant model — one-time catch-up `GRANT ALL` (tables/sequences/routines in `public`) for the 001–036 surface, plus `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES FOR ROLE postgres IN SCHEMA public` matching prod's `pg_default_acl` rows exactly (tables `arwdDxtm` / sequences `rwU` / functions `X` to anon/authenticated/service_role). Prod re-verified before building, per the task's stop condition: all 40 public tables (the full 001–036 surface — 36 is the count of applied migrations, not of tables; corrected per R2) grant all 8 table privileges (`arwdDxtm`, incl. PG17's MAINTAIN; "7" was a miscount, per R2) to the three API roles, and the default ACLs are exactly as D18 recorded. **ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES chosen over per-table grants** because production *is* a default-ACL model: with per-table grants only, any future migration that omitted a grant (by oversight or on purpose) would silently diverge from prod, which auto-grants regardless; the versioned default ACLs replay to prod's exact model in every environment — fresh local reset, and a future re-provisioned hosted project on the new no-auto-grant default. **[Corrected 2026-07-19 per R1 — the original entry claimed the migration was "pushed to prod itself"; it was not.]** What actually happened: 037 was applied locally only; the local grant/default-ACL state after a fresh reset was verified byte-identical to prod's (grants + `pg_default_acl` comparison). **037 has NOT been pushed to prod** — prod's migration history still ends at 036. It lands with the next normal migration push, where it is an idempotent no-op (prod already holds every grant and default ACL it writes). **Implication for every future leagues migration: no per-object GRANT statements — grants stay uniformly broad and RLS is the only effective gate (spec §12; §8.2's per-table pgTAP proves it). Deliberate narrowing (e.g. an internal service-role-only function) must be an explicit REVOKE in that migration.** *Carve-out (R5, 2026-07-19): "RLS is the only effective gate" holds for tables only — SECURITY DEFINER routines bypass RLS, so every one needs in-body auth (plan §8.3, spec §12.0) plus explicit REVOKE discipline; first instance `038_notify_list_followers_auth.sql`.* pgTAP `001_api_grants.sql` pins the surface (witness-table grants, default-ACL canary table + function, RLS-still-gates behavioral check) so a CLI behavior change fails loudly there. *Process note (R6, 2026-07-19): plan §8.1's staging-clone rehearsal line was skipped for 037 without a recorded waiver — recorded now: no staging clone exists (environments are local + prod only); the fresh local `db reset` replay plus the byte-identical grants/`pg_default_acl` comparison against prod served as the rehearsal evidence. Future schema-bearing migrations (039 `nfl_weeks` onward) must rehearse on a staging clone or record this waiver explicitly per migration.* Side effects: `nfl_weeks` renumbers 037 → 038 (tasks doc §5/§6 updated); typegen deliberately not re-run — grants change no schema shape, and regenerating would clobber the hand-written alias block in `src/types/database.ts`.

**2026-07-19 — Builder (L.A0.6):**
- **D29** R30's byte-identical source-semantics opt-in has its deliberate mechanism: `impersonateRecordedProvider(replay)`, exported beside `FixtureReplayProvider` — a loudly-named delegating wrapper whose `name` is the replayer's `recordedProviderName`. Impossible to reach silently, and asserted so in the gate: `FixtureReplayProvider` has no name option (every construction path yields the `fixture:`-prefixed name — pinned), the opt-in is a separate call at the composition site (never a flag that can default on), and a runtime `instanceof` guard refuses to relabel anything that isn't a real replayer (so it can't spoof arbitrary sources). Gate pins both modes on the real 2025-wk2 fixture: honest default writes `source: 'fixture:sleeper'`; the opt-in writes `source: 'sleeper'` with rows otherwise deep-equal (the D24 byte-identical guarantee).
- **D30** Gate composition mechanics (task L.A0.6; `src/lib/leagues/gate/`, run as `npm run test:gate`): (1) The multi-poll determinism fixture (`fixtures/nfl/2026/wk02/synthetic.jsonl.gz`, 220 entries) is **fully derived** — the pure `recordHappyPathSession()` (canonical 44-poll schedule: event-instant polls + 20-virtual-minute cadence across the Sunday slate + finalize/charted/correction-close beats, all inside the seeded wk2 bounds) is re-run in-process by the gate and compared **byte-for-byte** to the checked-in file, plus SHA-256 golden pins of both the serialized fixture and the full replayed upsert-batch sequence — a tampered fixture or an unversioned scenario-library change fails mechanically (extends the R23 contract to the fixture layer); regeneration after a deliberate version bump = `npm run record:synthetic-fixture`, updating the pins in the same commit. (2) Gate item 2c consumes the 039 seed by **parsing the checked-in migration SQL** — the (2026, 2) row regex-pinned with loud failure — zero external calls, asserting against the artifact that seeds every environment; scenario `correctionWindowEndsAt` equals the seeded instant exactly, all event instants + all 220 capture stamps inside `[starts_at, correction_window_ends_at]`, and the post-window correction dated beyond it. (3) Seam replays use **identity time-anchoring** (virtual clock constructed at the recording's first stamp) so `syncLiveStats`' calendar logic behaves exactly as it did live; the D11 wall-paced 1×/4×/64× slice runs against a D17 stub wall. (4) The 2025 real-week cross-check replays as a **finalize pass** (virtual now is months past the recorded `gameDate` window, so the gate seeds a lingering live week into the stateful capturing client) — settled finals written `is_live=false`, deterministic ×3. (5) R26 writer-side back-fill: run A lives through the `provider_outage` window on a 10-min cadence (4 failed polls write nothing; `DegradationTracker` flips at exactly the 3rd, clears on recovery), run B polls the same instants minus the outage window; A's first post-recovery upsert batch equals B's batch at the same instant and strictly advances past A's last pre-outage write — cumulative catch-up proven against the capturing client, not the provider.

**2026-07-20 — Builder (C15, pulled forward from M7 per Chris):**
- **D45** C15 hardening shipped as migration `048_secdef_search_path.sql` — the seven pre-017 search_path-less SECURITY DEFINER functions (`handle_new_user` [006 body], `update_follow_counts`/`update_list_player_count`/`update_tag_use_count`/`update_expert_follower_count` [001], `update_list_like_count` [003], `reorder_list_players` [004]) redefined with bodies **byte-identical** plus `SET search_path = public, pg_temp`. CREATE OR REPLACE preserves triggers + ACLs (004's REVOKE/GRANT on `reorder_list_players` untouched). §8.1 staging-rehearsal waiver recorded per the R6 rule (in the migration header): no staging clone exists; fresh local `db reset` over 001–048 + the pgTAP suite is the rehearsal evidence. Typegen not re-run (no schema shape change; regen would clobber the database.ts alias block). 048 is prod-safe as a routine push: same signatures, same logic, definitions replaced in place.

**2026-07-20 — Builder (L.A1.1):**
- **D49** Q4/Q7 implementation mechanics (migration `040_leagues_settings_columns.sql` + pgTAP `005`). (1) Username contract **folded into 040** (the ruling's offered option) — one migration, one reset proof; D36's 043 slot retired. (2) Persona pattern pinned as `^[a-z0-9]+(-[a-z0-9]+)*-ai$` (single-hyphen-separated lowercase alnum segments + `-ai`; all 9 live handles verified — vitest `username-contract.test.ts` pins every roster handle against BOTH patterns so a roster edit that would fail the DB constraint fails in vitest first). (3) The namespace guard is a **plain (non-SECURITY-DEFINER) trigger fn with `SET search_path = ''`** — 025's behavior pattern with narrower privileges; blocks persona-pattern usernames for `authenticated`/`anon` on INSERT/UPDATE-with-change; `auth.role()` is NULL in signup-trigger and seed contexts, so those paths are untouched. (4) Pre-checks are loud RAISE gates (contract violators listed; case-insensitive dups listed; leagues-rows-exist for the C9 default swap) — they double as the prod-push preconditions. (5) The `lower(username)` unique index is **redundant by construction** today (charset is lowercase-only) — kept as ruled defense-in-depth, noted in the banner. (6) `handle_new_user` deliberately unchanged: signup sends no username metadata, the fallback (`user_`+8hex) satisfies the human pattern, and the WHEN OTHERS handler means hostile metadata skips the profile without breaking the auth insert — both pinned behaviorally in pgTAP via real `auth.users` inserts. (7) pgTAP mechanics learned: `col_default_is` compares the *evaluated* default (not the deparsed `'x'::text`); data-modifying CTEs must be the top-level statement inside `results_eq` (003's form); `request.jwt.claims` set_config persists to txn end, so **every privileged-context test runs before any claims are set** (else `auth.role()` reads 'authenticated' and flips the guard on). (8) Browser pass caught a stale static hint ("3-30… must start with a letter") the validator edit alone would have missed; interactive verification of the auth-gated pages is limited (no dev one-click login; typing credentials is out of bounds) — noted in the PR. **(9) Review round (4 lenses, 13 findings — verifier fan-out died on a spend limit, so all 13 triaged inline; several were live-proven by the reviewers):** the **critical** one — GoTrue's public signup accepts `user_metadata.username`, and `handle_new_user` runs with `auth.role()` NULL, so an anonymous signup could mint `evil-ai` past the 040 guard (reproduced live) — fixed as **migration 049** (metadata honored only when human-pattern; must post-date 048, which re-creates the function; also upgrades invalid-metadata signups from profile-skipped to fallback-profile). Also fixed: `/username` was still a live rename surface post-selection → placeholder gate on mount + the UPDATE itself filtered to placeholder rows (Q7.2); index pin was name-only (a plain `(username)` index passed 46/46 — now `pg_get_indexdef ~ 'lower('`); guard test only covered single-segment `evil-ai` while every live persona is multi-segment (narrowed regex passed 46/46 — now `evil-twin-ai` pinned); 9 missing NOT NULL pins + 14 `col_type_is` added (drops/retypes passed 46/46 — proven); `count(*)` pins scoped to `pgtap-%` (future local leagues would false-red); guard trigger rewritten to 025's defensive TG_OP form (PG doesn't guarantee OR evaluation order over unassigned OLD); citext → `WITH SCHEMA extensions` (hosted advisor WARNs on public); C9 gate short-circuits when the canonical default is already in place (re-application safety); settings field drops `disabled` + `autoComplete` (copyability/AT access). pgTAP 005 grew 46 → **72** asserts. **Fact correction to Q7's premise:** the roster personas live in `ai_personas`, NOT `profiles` — the only persona-pattern profiles row is `fieldscout-ai` (system owner, service-role upsert; 049-compatible since the upsert overwrites the trigger fallback). The exemption + guard remain correct; the "9 prod persona profiles rows" claim was overstated.

**2026-07-21 — Builder (L.A1.8):**
- **D57** Calculator + derivation mechanics (`src/lib/leagues/scoring/calculator.ts` + `derive-stats.ts`, pure TS — no migration/pgTAP; the task prompt's "migration 058" parenthetical belongs to L.A1.9 per the corrected tasks-M1 §7 table, flagged in the PR). (1) **F20 discharged with the required semantics — cold buckets are DELIVERED-ZERO, no ruled alternative:** `deriveTierIndicators` emits the FULL def_pa/def_ya family as explicit 0/1 iff the family's raw source key (`def_points_allowed`/`def_yards_allowed`) is delivered; families deliver independently (PA without YA leaves only the YA rules keys pending); a derived family is fully authoritative — inbound def_pa_*/def_ya_* keys are stripped or recomputed, so an indicator can never appear without its source having been delivered this call. The pending path (§23.5/E61) stays reachable only for a genuinely unreported source. Badge semantics carry named tests that encode the specific wrong implementation (R56's falsifiability demand): an only-hot-bucket derive fails "normal-week derivation delivers the FULL family, cold buckets present-and-zero" plus the badge/pending-reachability tests — proven live as break probe 2 (41 tests fail), reverted. (2) **§7.3.3 "half-up" read as half-AWAY-FROM-ZERO** to match what `NUMERIC(8,2)` assignment does — identical on the positive domain the spec discusses; negative totals (all-miss K weeks) round like the DB column they land in (pinned at −0.005 → −0.01). Implementation snaps the scaled value via `toPrecision(13)` before `Math.round` so a true decimal .005 boundary rounds up instead of sitting on binary-float noise (0.335×3 → 1.01 pinned); break probe 1 (bankers' half-to-even) fails exactly the 3 rounding-pin tests, reverted. (3) **`perKey` is full-precision and delivered-keys-only** — `total` is the rounding of the full-precision sum, NOT Σ of rounded perKey entries (the spec's per-player rounding applies to the stored total); pending keys get no perKey entry at all, so a renderer cannot mistake pending for zero (E61). Defensive: non-finite stat/source values resolve as pending/unreported rather than poisoning a total. (4) **Bucket boundaries are explicit literals** in derive-stats.ts (reviewable), cross-checked BOTH directions against the registry by test: table keys ≡ the `storage:'derived'` key set, sizes pinned (11 PA = 7 shared + 4 ESPN, 9 YA), source keys pinned `storage:'column'`. `def_pa_0` = exactly 0; `def_ya_0_99` reads "<100" with an open lower bound (a negative-total-yards game belongs to it — tested at −12). Boundary instants tested at every bucket edge and one past; a one-hot-per-platform-set property runs PA 0–80 / YA −30–650 (the ESPN and shared PA sets share 4 keys by design — v2.8.3; the overlap double-pay footgun stays F21, v1.1). (5) **D33 one-namespace enforcement is mechanical, not honor-system:** the banned-literal list is computed as `Object.keys(STANDARD_SCORING)` minus the canonical registry keys (sanity-pinned non-empty), scanned against both module sources, plus an import-specifier regex ban on `scoring/default` (prose citations of the path stay legal — the ban targets imports). (6) Calculator is key-agnostic (imports nothing from the registry, mentions no concrete key — D33's "the calculator itself is key-agnostic"); only derive-stats and the tests touch STAT_KEYS. Property test uses the in-repo seeded mulberry32 (no Math.random, D3 spirit): 250 runs, total ≡ an independent sorted-iteration recompute, and adding an undelivered rules key never moves the total but surfaces as pending.

**2026-07-21 — Builder (M1 batch-5 findings remediation, R57–R61):**
- **D58** Scoring-module failure semantics for corrupt inputs (the two R58/R59 rulings, both pinned by test). **(1) R58 — an unmappable tier source value is UNREPORTED (family absent → pending), never an all-zero delivered family.** The decision hinged on whether the published bucket tables are real-contiguous (which would justify clamping/flooring a fractional value into a bucket) or integer-gapped: they are **integer-gapped** — ESPN's Scoring-Formats article prints PA `1–6 / 7–13 / 14–17 / …` and YA `100–199 / 200–299 / …` (re-verified value-for-value by the batch-4 review), leaving real-valued gaps between adjacent buckets (13.5, 99.5), so the tables' domain is the integers and there is no defensible bucket to clamp into. Consequence: `familyMaps` requires an INTEGER inside ≥1 bucket; a fractional value is out-of-domain even when it happens to sit inside an interval (14.5 pends the family, same as 13.5 — no partial acceptance of corrupt data), and a negative PA (below `def_pa_0`'s pinned floor) pends. Pinned at the exact review-probed gap values (PA=13.5, YA=99.5, negative PA, plus in-interval-fractional PA=14.5); the one-hot property extended beyond integers (fractional/negative/non-finite sweep) asserting *delivered ⇔ integer-in-a-bucket, and delivered ⇒ exactly one hot per platform set*; a named counter-pin proves the all-zero-family state (and partial-family emission) is unconstructible. **(2) R59 — a corrupt rules coefficient THROWS (TypeError naming every offending key), the loud option:** a non-finite-number coefficient in `scoring_rules_snapshot` is corrupt config, not missing data — marking it pending would misbadge corrupt config as "waiting on stats" (the reviewer's own analysis), and letting it through NaN-poisons a total `NUMERIC(8,2)` will store (Postgres numeric has NaN — live-verified in the review). Validation runs up-front over all rules keys (including keys with no delivered stat — corrupt config is never quietly parked as pending); string-numerics (`"0.5"`) throw too, since silent coercion masks the corruption the L.A1.11 freeze should have caught. Pinned: NaN can never reach the return value — every adversarial coefficient shape either throws TypeError or yields a finite total, plus a finite-total assertion inside the 250-run property. The M4 worker (L.D1) decides catch-and-quarantine vs fail-the-run at the call site (F23's composition row is where that lands); the module's contract is simply *never emit a poisoned or silently-wrong breakdown*. **(3) R61 taken:** the round(full sum)≠Σ(rounded perKey) rule now has a stored-literal discriminating pin (`{a:0.111,b:0.111}×{a:3,b:3} → 0.67`; Σ-of-rounded yields 0.66) beside the statistical property. **(4) R57's spec fold-back is v2.8.4** (§7.3.3 parenthetical + changelog; header Version field corrected 2.8.1 → 2.8.4 in passing — it had been stale since v2.8.2).

(6) **Auction solvency floor enforced when `draft.draft_type = 'auction'`** — the bullet is prefixed "Auction:"; a snake league's unused auction fields can't fail creation (pinned both ways). (7) Roster position vocabulary `QB/RB/WR/TE/K/DST/DL/LB/DB` — `DST` per the canonical JSONB shape (not the research-surface `DEF`); IR designations pinned to §7.3.2's six (OUT/IR/Doubtful/PUP/NFI/Suspended). (8) Schemas are **strict** (unknown keys rejected — typos fail loudly); `min_weeks` exists on restricted IR spots only (discriminated union; canonical `ir1` stays literal-exact), `label` optional on IR spots so `DL_PRESET` carries "DL" while `ir1` carries none; validator additionally enforces the cross-field R-column constraints the per-field schema can't (`playoff_teams ≤ team_count`; `trade_veto_votes ≤ team_count` when `league_vote`; `trade_deadline_week ≤ regular_season_weeks`) plus IR-key/eligible-set uniqueness. (9) **The 040 default cross-pin is mechanical, not copy-paste:** the test extracts the `::jsonb` roster literal from the checked-in `supabase/tests/005_leagues_settings_columns.sql` (the D30(2) parse-the-artifact pattern) and deep-equals it against `LEAGUE_SETTINGS_DEFAULTS.roster_settings`, beside a stored-literal golden pin of the FULL default object's serialization — drift in either home fails vitest. (10) The §7.3 round-trip **enumeration fixture is exported** (`round-trip-fixture.ts`: every field non-default except the three single-option fields — pinned as an exact set — with `scoring_system_id` carried alongside and pinned OUTSIDE `splitSettings`' output); L.A1.13 imports it rather than re-enumerating. (11) `mergeSettings` **throws** on corrupt rows (TypeError/ZodError — D58's loud-failure doctrine; a full generated row's extra fields are picked, never spread, so strictness can't false-trip). (12) **Observed, deliberately not improvised (→ F25; now §3 Q10 per R62):** §7.3.1's note says `playoff_start_week` is "validated against `regular_season_weeks`", but §7.3.8 has no such bullet and no formula is consistent with the printed ranges (strict `start = regular + 1` is unsatisfiable at `regular_season_weeks = 12` since start's floor is 14) — the validator implements exactly the task-enumerated §7.3.8 bullets.

Home: `leagues.creation_action_id UUID` + UNIQUE constraint; the route/hook stamp ONE UUID per user submit (the hook's `createLeague` wrapper stamps at call time so React Query retries reuse the same variables); the RPC replays a committed create (same payload, `replayed: true`, route maps to 200 vs 201) and converts the CONCURRENT double-submit race into a caught `unique_violation` → replay (constraint-name-dispatched; an invite-code collision retries generation instead). Double-submit pinned at BOTH layers per the task: service/route (same input twice → one row) and direct PostgREST rpc. A foreign user replaying someone else's action_id is refused (exact-message pin). (2) **splitSettings stays the §12.1 authority mechanically:** the RPC takes EVERY typed column as an explicit `p_<key>` arg + the blob as `p_settings`, and the service maps `splitSettings().columns` to args by key rename — a future typed column added in TS without a matching RPC arg fails LOUDLY at the PostgREST boundary (function-not-found), never lands silently in the blob; the RPC never re-splits or reshapes (pgTAP pins the stored blob byte-equal to the passed literal; vitest pins it equal to `splitSettings(input).blob`). `max_teams := p_team_count` in the same INSERT (§12.1 sync, first writer). (3) **§12.0 layering (059 precedent):** full §7.3.8 validation runs in the SERVICE (`validateLeagueSettings` — the API half of "enforced in API + DB constraints"); DB backstops are 040's CHECKs (team_count 23514 surfaces through the RPC — pinned), the D43 trigger (created leagues are born `setup` with a NULL snapshot — legitimately, pinned), and the in-body v1 templates-only check (`is_template = TRUE AND owner_id IS NULL`, both predicates checked so the guard survives a 058-CHECK change; friendly field-named message the route maps to a per-field 400; no-write pinned on rejection). The Q10 seam is live at this first persisting surface: the old overlap pair (15+14) drives the invalid-settings 400 pin, and the break probe (validation skipped in the service) shows it PERSISTING a league — failing exactly that test plus the downstream list count. *Annotation 2026-07-25 (R75 → D69): as first shipped this layering left the Q10 seam with NO DB-side backstop on the direct-RPC path — the batch-12 review live-proved an authenticated PostgREST caller persisting the overlap pair through `create_league` itself; the seam + 13–16 range are now in-body IFs, and the API-side-only scope of blob-shape validation is restated explicitly in the 060 banner. See D69.* (4) **`soft_delete_league` RPC shipped here** — the §15.1 DELETE verb's only spec-compliant writer (Q8/v2.8.2 removed all client DML; the ruling's own text names "a commish soft delete via RPC"): `is_league_commish` in-body (nonexistent league → same 42501, no existence leak — pinned), sets `deleted_at` only (CLAUDE.md rule 8), already-deleted → idempotent no-op success (D63 retry doctrine). No status restriction: §15.1 prints none and M1 leagues only sit in setup/scheduled. (5) **Held-lock <50ms (plan §8.3) cited INAPPLICABLE, not skipped:** create_league takes no locks on pre-existing rows — no FOR UPDATE/advisory locks (the double-submit race settles on the UNIQUE constraint), only inserts of rows invisible to concurrent txns; soft_delete_league holds the league row FOR UPDATE across exactly the one UPDATE the write requires. Recorded in the 060 banner. (6) **Service-layer testability structure:** Route Handlers are thin wrappers (auth + param plumbing — CLAUDE.md "app/ → minimal logic"); the injected-client service (`leagues-service.ts`) carries the whole composition (Zod → validate → split → RPC; RLS-scoped reads; mergeSettings with the D58 loud-throw surfaced as 500), so the stack suite drives the production composition over the real PostgREST wire path with real signed-in clients — the Next cookie layer is the only untested plumbing. Reads: list = `league_members` join (my leagues + role, deleted filtered); detail = RLS-gated league row (non-member → 404, no existence leak — pinned) + `mergeSettings` round-trip (detail settings deep-equal the submitted contract object — the create-path round-trip pinned wholesale; the per-field sweep stays L.A1.13/gate item 3) + members w/ profiles + teams + my_role. (7) **Free-create (Q6/v2.8, F5 create half) pinned in both suites** with the fixture's `is_pro` explicitly pinned FALSE first (a Pro fixture would make the pin vacuous); no `requireProUser`, no is_pro read anywhere in the path. (8) **team_name optional:** ''/whitespace → `"<display name>'s Team"` derived from the creator's profile (pinned in pgTAP); typegen can't express arg nullability, so the service passes '' rather than casting null. (9) pgTAP **014** (47 asserts) extends the 013 conventions (exact `search_path=""` pins, REVOKE ACL pins per role, exact-message golden pins, D49(7) claims ordering); pgTAP **005**'s exact-column pin updated for `creation_action_id` (the 055/invite_slug precedent). §4.2 has no new target (no new table; leagues policies unchanged since 054). Typegen re-run; alias block re-appended; diff verified additive-only (+28/−0).

**2026-07-25 — Builder (M1 batch-12 remediation, R75–R77):**
- **D69** Batch-12 remediation mechanics (same branch `feat/M1-L.A1.12-create-league`, PR #54 — fix-then-merge, no new PR; **060 amended in place** — unreleased per F12, prod history still ends at 036). (1) **R75 — Q10 seam DB backstop:** two in-body IFs in `create_league` between the templates-only check and the insert — the **13–16 range** (§7.3.1 R column) checked FIRST, then **strict continuity** `p_playoff_start_week = p_regular_season_weeks + 1` (Q10/v2.8.6) — both friendly field-named P0001s (the range-first order means a seam-consistent-but-out-of-range pair like 16+17 names the range). This is the DB half of §7.3.8's "enforced in API + DB constraints" for the seam: a direct-PostgREST caller never runs `validateLeagueSettings` (the Q8/R37–R38 lesson), and a persisted overlap pair poisons every read (D58 `mergeSettings` throw → GET detail 500s for all members). **Scope boundary restated (D68(3) stands):** blob-shape validation deliberately stays API-side; the backstop covers exactly the two typed-column seam invariants — recorded in the 060 banner so the boundary is explicit. Service: `playoff_start_week` marker added to `RPC_FIELD_ERRORS` so a surfaced DB refusal still maps to the same per-field 400 shape (on the service path the validator catches first — unchanged). pgTAP regression traps run the RPC DIRECTLY as `authenticated`: the exact live-proven bypass (15+14) → exact-message P0001 + no-write pin; range one-past both directions (12 via 11+12, 17 via 16+17 — seam-consistent so the RANGE half is what refuses, each check discriminated independently). (2) **R76 — replay honors `deleted_at`:** BOTH replay paths (the primary action_id lookup and the concurrent `unique_violation` replay) now refuse with a friendly terminal P0001 ("this create was already completed and the league has since been deleted — start a new league with a fresh submit") instead of replaying a soft-deleted league as success (which made the route 200 while GET detail 404s — live-proven in review); the HAPPY replay stays pinned pre-delete, so both sides of the `deleted_at` line are covered. (3) **R77 — top-level strict:** `createLeagueInputSchema` is `z.strictObject` (was plain `z.object`, which silently STRIPPED top-level unknown keys while the settings blob rejected them); vitest pins a top-level unknown key → 400 + no-write. (4) Counts: pgTAP 014 **47 → 53**; `leagues-api-db.test.ts` **15 → 16** (suite 483). **Break probe:** the seam IF removed from 060 → fresh reset → 014 fails EXACTLY the 2 R75 traps (37 "caught: no exception", 38 the overlap pair PERSISTED a row); reverted, full chain re-proven green after a fresh reset.
- **D55** Migration 056 mechanics (`056_league_weeks.sql` + pgTAP 010). (1) `league_weeks` RLS ships with **ONE** policy — member SELECT (`is_league_member`, not `is_league_commish` — unlike `league_invites`, §12.17 names no seat-targeted-privacy reason to narrow the read) — and no write policy for any role, including the commissioner: the task text says the table is "populated by M4's schedule/`league-week-advance` machinery," naming no client-write shape, so this follows `team_managers`'/`league_invites`' fully-RPC(-eventually-cron)-only precedent. (2) Two additive deviations from §12.17's printed DDL, both cheap and pinned, neither new product surface: (a) a CHECK enumerating `status`'s four values — §12.17 prints them only as a SQL comment, the exact R43 gap (`teams.status` silently accepted `'banana'` until 054 fixed it there); fixed here at creation instead of waiting for a review pass. Legal *transitions* between those four values are deliberately NOT enforced — no writer of any kind exists yet (RLS denies every client write; no RPC in this task calls one), so a transition trigger would have no legitimate caller to validate against until M4's `league-week-advance` job lands (D42/D53 scope-cut precedent). (b) `FOREIGN KEY (season, week) REFERENCES nfl_weeks(season, week)` — §12.17 prints season/week as unconstrained integers despite `nfl_weeks` (039/§12.20) being the spec's own calendar backbone (§23.3: "no code ever infers 'current week' from wall-clock math"); the composite FK is free (`nfl_weeks(season, week)` is already its PK, no new index needed) and makes a league week naming a nonexistent NFL week impossible. (3) pgTAP file is **010**, not the "009" tasks-M1's task text names — 009 was already taken by `league_invites` (L.A1.4, landed same session); same class of numbering drift already flagged elsewhere in that doc for migrations, now hit on the pgTAP sequence too. (4) Typegen: genuinely new table shape, full regen ran; `LeagueWeek` alias inserted alphabetically between `LeagueMember` and `List`; diff confirmed additive-only.

### Blocker

- **R1 · blocker · PROGRESS-leagues.md:104 (D23) + session log 2026-07-19 — refs: plan §2.3 DoD ("shown, not claimed"); CLAUDE.md Active Builds.** D23 records "The migration is an idempotent no-op pushed to prod itself" (session log: "local grants/default-ACLs verified byte-identical to prod") — but the hosted project's migration history, queried live this session, ends at `20260714171131 / player_usage_table` (= 036). **No 037 entry exists in prod.** Either the push never happened or it bypassed migration tracking; either way the decisions log asserts a prod state production contradicts, on a security-relevant migration. Until resolved, D23's premise (prod carries the versioned grant model) is unverified and the next `db push` will find 037 unapplied. Resolve by actually pushing 037 (recording it in prod's history) or correcting D23/the session log to what was actually done, with the verification evidence.

### Should-fix

**L.A0.5c — grants migration**

- **R2 · should-fix · PROGRESS:104 (D23) + 037_explicit_api_grants.sql:8 + commit 3d813ef — refs: tasks-M0 L.A0.5c stop condition.** The re-verification record miscounts its own surface: "all 36 public tables" — there are **40** (40 `CREATE TABLE`s, zero `DROP TABLE`s, in 001–036; 36 is the count of *applied migrations*) — and "all 7 privileges" contradicts the ACL D23 itself quotes (`arwdDxtm` = 8 bits incl. PG17 MAINTAIN). Compounds R1: the stop-condition verification record is internally inconsistent.
- **R3 · should-fix · 037:36–41 — refs: spec §12 ("RLS on every table"); spec §12.0 (REVOKE EXECUTE on league RPCs); plan §8.1–8.2; D23.** The default-ACL model flips the failure mode fail-closed → fail-open: pre-037, a forgotten grant produced a loud 42501; now a future migration that forgets `ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` ships a table on which anon holds full privileges, silently. No automated backstop landed. A one-line pgTAP global assertion in 001_api_grants.sql (e.g. `is_empty($$ select tablename from pg_tables where schemaname='public' and not rowsecurity $$)`) would machine-enforce spec §12; the same fail-open logic will apply to §12.0's per-RPC REVOKEs.
- **R4 · should-fix · supabase/tests/001_api_grants.sql:30 — refs: D23 ("pins the surface"); tasks-M0 L.A0.5c item 3.** The pin has holes: the canary table (`id int primary key`) creates no sequence and there is no `has_sequence_privilege` assertion, so 037's sequence catch-up grant *and* sequence default ACL have zero coverage — deleting 037 lines 29 and 38–39 still passes 10/10. No existing-routine catch-up witness either (e.g. the 004 reorder RPC), and the table/function checks sample SELECT/INSERT/EXECUTE rather than the full privilege set D23 claims to pin.
- **R5 · should-fix · 037:30 + 019_list_update_notifications.sql:18 — refs: spec §12.0; plan §8.3; D23.** D23's doctrine "grants stay uniformly broad and RLS is the only effective gate" is false for routines: SECURITY DEFINER bodies bypass RLS. 037 versions anon EXECUTE on `notify_list_followers(p_list_id, p_actor)` — SECURITY DEFINER, zero `auth.*` checks in the body, caller-supplied `p_actor` — which can insert notifications attributed to arbitrary users. The exposure pre-exists under the legacy model, but the migration now codifies it; the doctrine needs a SECURITY DEFINER carve-out, and future league RPCs need §8.3 in-body auth + explicit REVOKE discipline.
- **R6 · should-fix · 037 (process) — refs: plan §8.1 ("staging-clone rehearsal noted"); plan §2.3.** §8.1's rehearsal line is silently skipped: no note in the migration, D23, the session log, or commit 3d813ef, and no waiver covers it (contrast D10/D23, which waive other checklist lines explicitly).

**L.A0.5a — pgTAP bootstrap**

- **R7 · should-fix · supabase/tests/000_harness_smoke.sql:38 — refs: plan §8.2; D19.** The read-side "behavioral half" is `lives_ok(select count(*))`, which cannot fail for the reason it claims to test: a policy qual rewritten to `using (false)` (keeping the name and SELECT cmd, so `policies_are`/`policy_cmd_is` still pass) leaves the suite 6/6 green while anon reads return nothing. Assert visible rows (`results_eq`/nonzero count against seed data) to make the read half falsifiable.

**L.A0.2a — StatsProvider contract**

- **R8 · should-fix · src/lib/leagues/stats/stat-keys.ts:92 — refs: spec §23.5 (core_box = App B categories); App B.1/B.4 (`dst_model` split for ESPN); D21; CLAUDE.md spec-question rule.** The ESPN split D/ST points/yards-allowed model — core_box, required by 2 of the 6 v1 parity templates — has zero registry keys, and no decision or filed spec question covers the omission (D21 names middle tiers for the *single* model only; the Q2/v2.7 punt covers B.2–B.3, not core_box D/ST). The spec names no canonical split-model keys, so this needed a D21-style naming decision or a §3 Spec Question; as-is, M1's ESPN template-parity work lands on a gap.
- **R9 · should-fix · stat-keys.test.ts:96 + stat-keys.ts:25 — refs: D20; tasks-M0 L.A0.2a item 4.** D20's "typed `keyof` the generated Row so a bogus storage mapping fails type-check" overstates the guard: `keyof Row` only rejects *non-existent* columns — a wrong-but-existing column (`fg_40_49` → `fg_made_50_plus`, `pat_made` → `xp_attempted`) passes type-check and all tests, since the only column assertion is `toBeDefined()`. The two divergence-documented mappings are exactly the swap-prone ones and no fixture pins any key→column pair. Add a pinning fixture before L.A0.2b wires consumers.
- **R10 · should-fix · sleeper-stats-provider.ts:63 — refs: D22; tasks-M0 L.A0.2a item 3.** D22's rule "maps only repo-evidenced Sleeper fields (live-stats `STAT_MAP` + `SleeperProjectedStats`)" is false for `xpmiss → pat_missed`: neither named source contains `xpmiss`; its only prior repo occurrence is a *projections*-endpoint test fixture — a different endpoint from the `/stats` actuals this adapter polls. If the actuals endpoint spells it differently, `pat_missed` silently never reports.
- **R11 · should-fix · sleeper-stats-provider.test.ts:118 — refs: plan §2.3; tasks-M0 L.A0.2a item 4.** The adapter↔registry cross-check iterates the implementation's own map values, so a silently dropped mapping (delete `rec_td` or `rush_2pt`) fails nothing — no happy-path fixture even contains a WR/TE row. The test proves "everything mapped is canonical," never "everything expected is mapped." Assert the expected key set explicitly.
- **R12 · should-fix · sleeper-stats-provider.ts:81 — refs: spec §23.2; tasks-M0 L.A0.4.** `mapSleeperGameStatus`'s recognized strings (`completed`/`post_game`/`in_game`/`in_progress`) have no repo evidence (pre-existing code never branches on schedule status), the test mirrors the implementation's own strings, and the `completed`/`in_progress` branches are unasserted — a wrong guess silently downgrades live/final games to the `scheduled` fallback until the September recorder produces real data.
- **R13 · should-fix · sleeper-stats-provider.ts:119 — refs: spec §23.2 ("never wrong numbers"); D22.** The weekly-stats response is an unvalidated `as SleeperWeeklyStatsRow[]` cast with no known-player filter (unlike live-stats.ts): a malformed row missing `player_id` flows through as `playerId: undefined` in a field typed `string`.
- **R14 · should-fix · stat-keys.test.ts:103 — refs: D15; tasks-M0 L.A0.3.** Deleting both D15 placeholder keys passes the whole suite — every placeholder assertion iterates *existing* entries; nothing asserts any tracking/charted key exists. L.A0.3's scenario library depends on their presence.

**L.A0.1 — TimeProvider**

- **R15 · should-fix · .eslintrc.json:10 — refs: D3 ("mechanical, not convention"); CLAUDE.md (TimeProvider-only time).** Empirically probed: the two selectors miss lint-clean wall-clock routes inside `src/lib/leagues/**` — bare `Date()` (a genuine current-time read one token from the banned form), `globalThis.Date.now()`, `const D = Date; D.now()`, `Date["now"]()`, `new Date(...args)`, and `performance.now()`. The two named forms are caught; the mechanical guarantee is narrower than D3 records.
- **R16 · should-fix · src/lib/leagues/time/virtual-clock.ts:47 — refs: tasks-M0 L.A0.1 item 3 ("advanceTo monotonic (throws on backwards)").** `advanceTo(new Date('bogus'))` passes the `targetMs < currentMs` guard (NaN comparisons are always false), anchors the clock at NaN, and every subsequent genuinely-backwards target *also* fails to throw — the documented monotonic contract is silently and permanently disabled. `advanceBy` has the matching `Number.isFinite` guard; `advanceTo` lacks it, and no test covers an Invalid Date target.
- **R17 · should-fix · .eslintrc.json:5 vs PROGRESS:77 — refs: D3.** D3 records the lint ban as covering `src/lib/leagues/**` **+ `src/lib/sync/live-stats.ts`**, but the shipped override targets only the former. Presumably the live-stats guard waits for L.A0.2b's seam refactor (the file still legitimately reads wall time today) — but nothing records that deferral, so the decisions log overstates the shipped guard. Extend the override at L.A0.2b or annotate D3 with the deferral.

### Nit

- **R18 · nit · time-provider.test.ts:8 — refs: tasks-M0 §4 (systemTime = wall clock).** The sole `systemTime` test passes for a frozen implementation (`now: () => new Date(0)`) — it asserts instance/finiteness, never that the value tracks the system clock (`vi.setSystemTime` would make it falsifiable lint-clean).
- **R19 · nit · 037:28 — refs: spec §12; D23.** "RLS is the effective gate" is not literally true for TRUNCATE (row security does not apply to it), which line 28 grants anon/authenticated on every table. No PostgREST path issues TRUNCATE — hence nit — but `REVOKE TRUNCATE` from the API roles is exactly the "deliberate narrowing" 037's own header contemplates.
- **R20 · nit · tasks-M0-foundations.md:34 — refs: D23 (renumbering).** §2's "next = **037**" survey line was left stale by the renumbering pass that updated §5/§6.
- **R21 · nit · package.json (devDependencies) — refs: D19.** D19's "CLI pinned (`supabase@2.109.1`)" is actually caret `^2.109.1`. The committed lockfile pins installs in practice, but a lockfile regen or `npm update` drifts silently — an exact pin is the robust reading of D19 given the known 2026-10-30 CLI behavior change.
