## Review findings — 2026-07-19 (batch 2: L.A0.5b / L.A0.2b / L.A0.3 / L.A0.4)

*Reviewer session (fresh context, red-team brief): diff `0a71091..HEAD` (merges #12–#15) — verified against spec v2.7 (§12.20, §23.1–23.6), delivery plan §2.3/§8, and decisions D24–D28. Method: every load-bearing claim re-verified by hand — full vitest re-run (122/122 green), lint + type-check re-run (clean; one pre-existing warning in `auction-draft-room.tsx`, outside this diff), ESLint stdin probes against the determinism guard, and the 2026 `nfl_weeks` seed re-derived against the live Sleeper schedule endpoint the banner cites. Review-only — nothing fixed. Findings R22–R30; **no blockers**.*

**Verified sound (claims audited and confirmed):**

- **RETURNING-count RLS deny pattern (003_nfl_weeks.sql) is sound, not masking.** The layering closes every hole I could construct: a permissive UPDATE/DELETE policy flips the count 0→18 and fails; `policies_are` catches a `USING (false)` decoy write policy by name; RLS-disabled trips both the count tests (18 affected) and the 001 global backstop; and the "0 rows because the seed is missing" mask is excluded by the same-role SELECT-sees-18 pin immediately before each deny block. INSERT correctly expects 42501 (the one write RLS raises on rather than filters).
- **2026 seed verified against its claimed source.** Live Sleeper schedule fetch: week 1's first game IS Wed 2026-09-09, week 12 IS the second Wednesday slate (Nov 25) — the banner's claims are true, and all 18 `starts_at` rows equal "Wednesday 00:00 ET preceding the week's first game" for the fetched slate (incl. wk18's Sun-only slate → Wed 2027-01-06). DST handling correct: Nov 1 2026 fall-back puts wk8's window at −05 while its start is −04, wk9 onward −05/−05; every correction window is the following Thursday 06:00 ET per §23.4.
- **Plan §8.1/§8.2 pass literally for 039** with both waivers now recorded in-banner (D10 realtime; §8.1 rehearsal per the R6 go-forward rule); typegen committed with the hand-written alias block preserved (memory risk avoided).
- **Boundary-instant pins are real where claimed:** synthetic beats pin T−1s AND T exactly for kickoff, inactives publication (kickoff−90m), injury report, charted post, correction instant, SLA slip, revision instant, and DegradationTracker's exactly-3rd-poll flip; replay pins latest-≤-now at the exact capture stamp (3:59 vs 4:00). An off-by-one (`>` vs `>=`) at any of these instants fails a test.
- **Record→replay equality WOULD catch broken Date revival:** the round-trip transcript compares live `Date` objects against revived bodies with `toEqual`, which distinguishes `Date` from ISO string; a dedicated `instanceof` + gameDate-stays-string pin backs it up. D27 parse rejects unknown format and version loudly (both asserted).
- **D25 holds:** `gameDate` is consumed only by the seam's window check (`live-stats.ts:159`), exactly the pre-seam day-math; nothing treats it as a kickoff. **D24 column surface holds:** registry-derived writer surface = the 27 pre-seam STAT_MAP columns exactly (pinned by test), 2-pt summing byte-identical, string-coercion semantics preserved via the adapter's `Number()` path.
- **D28 opt-in is safe:** nothing in the diff can write byte-identical source semantics without deliberately constructing a wrapper — `FixtureReplayProvider.name` is always `fixture:<source>`; see R30 for the flip side.

### Should-fix

- **R22 · should-fix · .eslintrc.json:50 — refs: D3/R15 ("mechanical, not convention"); D26 item 5.** The new `Math.random` guard did not inherit the R15 widening the Date guard got. Empirically probed via ESLint stdin against `src/lib/leagues/**`: `globalThis.Math.random()` and `const M = Math; M.random()` are both **lint-clean** — the exact two escape shapes (globalThis routing, constructor/object aliasing) R15 closed for `Date`. `crypto.getRandomValues(...)` is also unbanned (a common "random" reach). D26 records "two new D3-style lint selectors," but the shipped guard is strictly narrower than the D3 guard it cites. Add `MemberExpression[object.name='globalThis'][property.name='Math']`, Math-aliasing selectors, and (optionally) a crypto.getRandomValues ban.
- **R23 · should-fix · synthetic-stats-provider.test.ts:128 + scenarios.ts:21 — refs: §23.6 ("versioned, deterministic, re-runnable"); D26 (library versioning).** No test pins any concrete PRNG-derived stat value against a stored golden constant. Both determinism tests compare two in-process runs of the same code (`fullWeekTranscript` twice; outage vs happy_path), so an *unintended* behavior change — a draw-order refactor in `finalLine`, a range tweak, a `mulberry32` edit — changes every scenario's "deterministic" world while the entire suite stays green. That defeats the versioning contract the schema declares ("bump `version` on ANY behavior change"): nothing can detect an unversioned behavior change, and any future fixture/simulator run pinned against library v1 silently diverges. One golden test (e.g. the full DEFAULT_SEED stat line for one player per position, as literals) makes version bumps mechanical instead of honor-system.
- **R24 · should-fix · live-stats.test.ts:40 — refs: the falsifiability standard this batch claims throughout; §23.2.** The seam's game-window boundary is the one instant-sensitive edge in this diff with **no** boundary pin: tests run mid-window (18:00Z on game day) and 3 days out, never at `kickoffDay` exactly or `kickoffDay + 36h` exactly (live-stats.ts:163 — both edges inclusive). An off-by-one in either comparison (`>=`→`>`, `<=`→`<`) passes the suite. Two exact-instant cases close it; the window semantics are load-bearing for the finalization pass (`weeksToFinalize` gets `inWindow` from this check).

### Nit

- **R25 · nit · synthetic-stats-provider.ts:249 — refs: D25 ("provider-reported calendar day").** Synthetic derives `gameDate` from the kickoff's **UTC** day, so the SNF game (Sun 8:20pm ET) reports Monday `2026-09-21`, while the sleeper tier reports the ET day (checked-in 2025 fixture: Sunday games carry the Sunday date). Literally D25-compliant but a cross-tier semantic drift in the same field: composed with the seam's UTC-day-start+36h window it shifts a late game's window by a day vs. real data. Harmless on the current slate (earlier games open the window); worth aligning to the ET day before L.A0.6 composes synthetic with the seam.
- **R26 · nit · scenario-beats.test.ts:290 (D26 back-fill proof) — refs: §23.2 (back-fill on recovery).** Answering the brief directly: the outage-vs-happy_path equality is genuinely cross-scenario (it would catch PRNG streams keyed by scenario id, or outage state leaking into values — the two ways this provider *could* regress), but it is **not an independent computation**: both sides run the identical pure `finalLine`/`progress` code on identical inputs, and since the provider is stateless the equality is near-tautological. What §23.2 actually cares about — writer-side cumulative back-fill after a degraded window (upserts catching up in `player_stats`) — is untested anywhere in this batch and lands on L.A0.6. Fine for M0 provider scope; recorded so the gate task doesn't inherit "back-fill is proven" as a premise.
- **R27 · nit · scripts/record-fixtures.ts:90,99 — refs: D27 (identity lives IN the artifact).** The CLI hardcodes `provider: 'sleeper'` in the header and `sleeper.jsonl.gz` in the path instead of reading `provider.name` — re-introducing at write time exactly the guessing D27 exists to prevent. Equal today; a silent lie the day the CLI wraps any other provider.
- **R28 · nit · fixture-format.ts:68 — refs: D27 ("parse-rejected loudly on mismatch").** Loud rejection covers `format`/`version` only; a header missing `provider`/`season`/`week` parses fine and yields `name = 'fixture:undefined'` downstream. A three-field presence check would make the identity guarantee total.
- **R29 · nit · fixture-replay-provider.ts:104 — refs: D6 (failures replay as failures).** Recorded failures replay as a generic `Error` carrying the message only — the recorded `status` (e.g. 429 vs 500) is dropped, so a future consumer branching on status sees a different shape live vs replay. No consumer branches today.
- **R30 · nit · D24/D28 seam composition (live-stats.ts:94 + fixture-replay-provider.ts:58).** D24's "record/replay reproduces today's writes" is true for every column *except* `source`: the writer stamps `provider.name`, and replay's name is `'fixture:sleeper'`, so a replayed ingestion writes `source: 'fixture:sleeper'`. That is D28's intended honest default — but the documented opt-in (`recordedProviderName`) has **no supported mechanism** reaching the writer; L.A0.6 will have to hand-roll a delegating wrapper that overrides `name` to get byte-identical rows. Deliberate-by-design per D28; recorded so the gate builder budgets for it (and so the wrapper, when written, is recognized as the sanctioned deliberate-impersonation path, not a smell).

| R35 (nit, taken) | `285720d` — persona branch gains `char_length <= 32` + loud pre-check, folded into 050 (the finding's own trigger: "whenever the constraint is next touched"); 32/33 boundary pins |
| R36 (nit, taken) | `7be6b0a` — Q7 record annotated in place with the D49(9) `ai_personas` fact correction |

- **R31 · should-fix · supabase/migrations/049_handle_new_user_username_contract.sql:67 — refs: Q7.1, §7.2 (AI-transparency namespace).** The fallback path launders the rejected metadata username into `display_name`: a signup with `user_metadata.username = 'evil-ai'` (no `full_name`) gets username `user_xxxxxxxx` but **`display_name = 'evil-ai'`** — reproduced live (rolled-back probe). The `COALESCE(full_name, new_username, 'New User')` should use `effective_username` (or skip `new_username` when it failed validation). Materiality caveat: `display_name` is already freely user-editable to any string via settings, so this adds no capability an attacker lacks — but the trigger shouldn't propagate input it just rejected into another league-rendered field, and it makes the 049 banner's "hostile metadata falls back" claim only half-true. (The wider fact that display names can impersonate the personas' "(AI)" convention is pre-existing and out of the Q7 contract's scope — usernames only. Flag for a future ruling if display-name spoofing ever matters.)
- **R32 · should-fix · src/app/(auth)/username/page.tsx:18–26,122 — refs: v2.8.1 Q7.2 ("permanent after explicit selection").** The placeholder shape is not reserved: `user_deadbeef` is a *valid human username* (13 chars, `[a-z0-9_]`) that also matches `PLACEHOLDER_REGEX` — a user who selects it stays "pre-selection" forever: the mount gate never routes them out and the filtered UPDATE keeps matching, so `/username` itself becomes an unlimited sanctioned rename surface for that account. `validateUsername` should reject `^user_[0-9a-f]{8}$` (and the eventual server-side selection RPC should reserve the shape at the DB layer). Low severity in practice (self-inflicted, human-pattern names only — the persona namespace is unaffected), but it falsifies "permanent after explicit selection" as stated.
- **R33 · nit · supabase/tests/005_leagues_settings_columns.sql:17–18 — refs: 049 banner.** The header's falsifiability note still carries the pre-049 wording ("hostile metadata **skips the profile** WITHOUT breaking the auth insert") — the tests below it pin the opposite (049's fallback profile). Stale comment only; the asserts are right.
- **R34 · nit · supabase/tests/005_leagues_settings_columns.sql:236–263 — refs: §8.2 ("pgTAP negative test per role").** The namespace-guard denials run as `authenticated` only; the `anon` branch of `auth.role() IN ('authenticated','anon')` is never exercised. One IN-list makes divergence implausible today, but a future guard rewrite that splits the roles would lose `anon` coverage silently. (Direct anon INSERT is separately RLS-blocked — probe shown.)
- **R35 · nit · supabase/migrations/040_leagues_settings_columns.sql:163 — refs: Q7.1.** The persona CHECK branch has no length bound (human is 5–20; a persona handle could be arbitrarily long). Privileged-writers-only, all live handles ≤17 chars, so cosmetic — worth a cap whenever the constraint is next touched.
- **R36 · nit · PROGRESS §3 Q7 (lines ~94) — record integrity.** The Q7 entry still asserts the roster personas exist as profiles rows "in production" (~19 rows) with no inline pointer to the D49(9) fact correction (they live in `ai_personas`; only `fieldscout-ai` is a profiles row). History shouldn't be rewritten, but a one-line *[corrected — see D49(9)]* annotation would keep a §3-only reader from re-deriving the wrong prod precondition for the 040 pre-checks.

### Should-fix

- **R37 · should-fix · 052:127 (kept "League owners can manage") + 040 (`scoring_rules_snapshot`, `status`, `settings` columns) — refs: spec §8.1 (server-authoritative), §7.3.3/§7.3.8, §12.1 ("keep"), CLAUDE.md never-weaken (snapshot reads); recommend a §3 spec question.** 001's `FOR ALL USING (auth.uid() = owner_id)` policy — which §12.1 explicitly says to keep, and 052 duly kept — now composes with 040's columns into a client-writable league-state surface: **live-proven this session**, an ordinary authenticated user INSERTs a `leagues` row directly, then UPDATEs `scoring_rules_snapshot = '{"forged":true}'` and `status = 'drafting'` in one statement (they cannot seed themselves into `league_members` — the commish bootstrap is closed). Consequences: the templates-only rule's in-body RPC enforcement (the breakdown review's critical fix) is bypassable by direct PostgREST DML; D43's future lifecycle trigger only raises on a NULL snapshot, so forge-snapshot-then-transition sails past it; the owner can also hard-DELETE the league (rule-8 soft-delete bypass), cascading `league_members` — and via `league_id` — `team_managers` rows away (blocked only once league `teams` rows exist, since `teams.league_id` is NO ACTION). Nothing in this batch is wrong against the spec — the spec's own "keep" instruction creates the hole — so per CLAUDE.md this needs a filed spec question (recommendation: replace the owner FOR ALL with owner-manage scoped to safe columns/lifecycle, or guard triggers on `scoring_rules_snapshot`/`status`/`deleted_at`; must be ruled before L.A1.11–L.A1.13 land, where the enforcement-point claims would otherwise be false).
- **R38 · should-fix · 052:114–117 ("Commish manages members", spec-printed) — refs: §12.2 v2.1 note (same-txn cache⇄stint update), §12.22, D51(3).** The commish FOR ALL policy lets a commissioner client-UPDATE `league_members.team_id`/`role` via PostgREST — a reseat that writes the current-state **cache** with no `team_managers` stint, silently diverging cache from history (the exact gap §7.2.1 exists to prevent; 006's test 62 demonstrates the write path works). `team_managers`' no-client-write posture protects the history rows themselves but not the invariant. Spec-printed policy, so same disposition as R37: fold into the spec question (narrow the commish policy's write surface, or accept that L.A1.15's RPCs are advisory for commissioners and the cache is untrusted).
- **R39 · should-fix · 053:93–94 + 052:79–80 — refs: plan §8.1 ("indexes for every FK used in policies/joins").** Checklist literally unmet: `team_managers.league_id` has **no index** (it is the SELECT-policy argument and every league-history query's filter); `team_managers.team_id` is covered only by the partial `one_open_stint_per_team` (`WHERE ended_at IS NULL`) — closed-stint franchise-history reads and the `teams` ON DELETE CASCADE check scan; `league_members.team_id` has no leading-column index (the `teams` ON DELETE SET NULL path scans; `UNIQUE(league_id, team_id)` doesn't serve it). §12.2/§12.22 verbatim name only the shipped indexes — spec silence vs checklist requirement; an additive index migration resolves both readings.
- **R40 · should-fix · 053:60 (`successor_team_id`) — refs: §7.2.1 (retire-and-succeed semantics), §12.22; probed live.** A team is accepted as its own successor, and a two-team successor cycle is accepted — nothing at the DB layer constrains the succession chain (spec-verbatim: §12.22 prints no constraint). Writes are server-only, but the retire RPC that would validate this is M4-deferred (D42), so the gap has no scheduled owner. `CHECK (successor_team_id <> id)` is free now; cycle rejection belongs in `retire_franchise` — record the hand-off so it isn't lost.
- **R41 · should-fix · PROGRESS §2 checklist rows L.A1.4/L.A1.5/L.A1.7/L.A1.9/L.A1.11 — record integrity.** The rows still carry migrations **043/044/045/046/047**; the corrected tasks-M1 §7 table (and this file's own L.A1.3 session-log row: "→ migration **054**") says **054/055/056/057/058**. A fresh session reading §2 — the canonical "immediate next work" — inherits the stale reservation the renumbering was supposed to retire.

### Nit

- **R42 · nit · 052:28–35 (header) + D50(2) + 006:19–21 — refs: 037/D23 grant model.** The deviation text says "revoking anon EXECUTE would turn every anon SELECT ... into a 42501" — probed: a literal `REVOKE EXECUTE ... FROM anon` is a **silent no-op** (006 stays 71/71 green) because the load-bearing grant is Postgres's default `PUBLIC` EXECUTE (`=X` in proacl), which anon inherits. The pin only trips (loudly — suite aborts) under the house form `REVOKE ... FROM PUBLIC, anon` (038/004 precedent), which is the realistic over-eager migration, so the falsifiability claim holds in practice — but the header and D50 should name PUBLIC as the mechanism.

Spec-verbatim (§12.22 prints none) and writes are server-only — cheap defense whenever these columns are next touched.

## Review findings — 2026-07-20 (M1 batch 3)

*Reviewer session (fresh context, red-team brief): diff `09786b4..6bacadb` (merges #28, #30) — L.A1.4 (migration 055: `league_invites` + `invite_slug`, pgTAP 009) and L.A1.5 (migration 056: `league_weeks`, pgTAP 010) — verified against spec v2.8.2 §12.23/§12.17, erratum v2.7.1 (D46/D48), plan §8.1–8.3, decisions D53–D55. Method: fresh local-stack baseline (`test:db` **401/401** green, matching the claimed counts) before any probing; live DML-level boundary probes run against the local stack (each inside a rolled-back transaction — nothing persisted); DDL-level break-probe re-derivation done statically (see falsifiability note below — the sandbox's permission classifier blocked both raw policy/constraint DDL via `psql` and `supabase db reset` for this session, so the two probes below are line-cited logical re-derivations against the shipped pgTAP assertions, not fresh live executions — flagged so this isn't miscounted as "shown" under this project's own standard). Review-only — nothing fixed. **No blockers.**

**Verified clean (for the record):**

- **§12.23/§12.17 column-for-column, exactly.** `055_league_invites.sql` matches the spec's printed DDL (`docs/specs/spec-redraft-leagues.md:1282-1304`) column-for-column, plus exactly the two authorized additive pieces: the D46 send-tracking pair (`created_at NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()`, `last_sent_at` nullable — erratum v2.7.1 item 4) and nothing else. `056_league_weeks.sql` matches §12.17's printed DDL (`spec:1139-1153`) column-for-column, plus exactly the two D55-recorded additions: the `status` CHECK enumerating the four values the spec only comments, and `FOREIGN KEY (season, week) REFERENCES nfl_weeks(season, week)`. pgTAP 009/010's `columns_are` pins assert the exact column sets (009:50-54, 010:42-45) — no undocumented drift in either migration.
- **The general multi-use league link is schema-complete and ungated.** No CHECK or policy in 055 ties `invited_email`/`invited_username` to anything — both are independently nullable with no constraint requiring either. A row with `target_team_id`, `invited_username`, AND `invited_email` all NULL (the unrestricted/open-link case) is fully insertable and, being unconstrained, remains claimable-by-anyone at the schema level — confirmed by direct constraint inspection (`pg_constraint` on `league_invites`: only FKs + the `token` UNIQUE + PK, no CHECK at all). Separately, `leagues.invite_code`/`invite_slug` (the persistent per-league link the Q5 "always visible and copyable" bar actually rests on, per `tasks-M1-league-foundation.md:341`) is **not** reachable via a direct client SELECT on `leagues` today — the 052 policy swap (`is_league_member(id) OR owner_id = auth.uid()`) never carried an invite-code carve-out, before or after 055/056 — but this is by design, not a regression: the join flow's own plan (`tasks-M1:284,350`) routes every pre-auth resolution (seat token → invite_code → invite_slug) through `get_join_preview`, a planned `SECURITY DEFINER` RPC with a documented anon-`EXECUTE` carve-out, which bypasses `leagues` RLS entirely by construction. Nothing in 055/056 narrows or blocks that plan; it's simply not built yet (tracked as L.A1.14, already on the checklist — see the ledger gap below for the one piece of it that isn't tracked as sharply as it should be).
- **Privacy posture on `league_invites` holds.** The only policy touching the table anywhere in 001–056 is `"Invites viewable by commish"` (`is_league_commish`-scoped SELECT) — grepped for every migration and every function/view; nothing else references `league_invites`. pgTAP 009 counter-pins a non-commish member (sees 0), a non-member (sees 0), and cross-league (a commish sees 0 of another league's invites) — independently re-derivable from the policy predicate alone (no `OR` branch exists for `invited_email = auth.email()` or `claimed_by = auth.uid()`). `invited_email` is therefore unreachable pre-claim by anyone but the commissioner reading the row directly; D48 additionally commits the not-yet-built preview RPC to return only league name + team label + inviter display name (no email). Confirmed clean.
- **Record integrity.** D54 (`PROGRESS:215`) matches `055_league_invites.sql` exactly (RLS shape, CITEXT-first claim, `expires_at`-vs-`created_at` boundary framing); D55 (`PROGRESS:218`) matches `056_league_weeks.sql` exactly (RLS shape, the two additive deviations, the pgTAP-009-already-taken numbering note). The 055→059 renumbering is consistent everywhere checked: PROGRESS §2's checklist, `tasks-M1-league-foundation.md`'s §7 table and per-task headers, and the decisions log all agree on 055/`league_invites` and 056/`league_weeks` — no stale "043"/"044" references survive for these two. The branch+PR rule (CLAUDE.md Active Builds, added `3d82493`) is fully respected in the visible history: `git log --first-parent` on `main` is a pure chain of "Merge pull request" commits, and both 055's commit (`8f16707`) and 056's commit (`ed2e94f`) sit on feature branches (`feat/M1-L.A1.4-league-invites`, `feat/M1-L.A1.5-league-weeks`) merged via PR #28/#30 — neither landed on `main` directly, before or after the rule existed.
- **`league_weeks.status`'s CHECK is real defense-in-depth, not just a client-facing gate.** Live-probed as the Postgres superuser (RLS-bypassing) role: `INSERT ... status = 'banana'` still throws `23514`. Unlike RLS, the CHECK constraint is enforced regardless of role — meaning it will still catch a buggy M4 cron job (`league-week-advance`) writing this table in a privileged/`SECURITY DEFINER` context, not only a hypothetical client. Worth having on record since no writer of this column exists yet to have exercised that path.

### Resolution — 2026-07-20 (Builder, branch `fix/M1-batch3-review-findings-R47-R48`)

*Both findings resolved, docs-only — no migration, no schema change (rationale below). No proof run owed: nothing executable changed; the pgTAP suite's 401/401 baseline from the review session stands.*

| Findings | Resolved by |
|---|---|
| R47 | this commit — **§6 Forward obligations ledger** added (18 entries, F1–F18) seeded from a full sweep of the D/R/Q records: the four R47-named scatterees (D53 retire cycle, D48 preview exposure, R46 stint hand-off, D55 transition trigger) plus the Q6 free-user negative tests and 13 more found sweeping (D7/D10/D16/D23-R1/D38/D40/Q2-D15/Q3/Q5-D37/Q7.2/R19/C11-C12). Header states the discharge rule: builders MUST flip a listed obligation in the same PR that builds its task; reviewers audit every batch |
| R48 | **Ledger entry F6, NOT a migration — Builder judgment call as delegated.** Why not DB CHECKs now: **(a)** the mutual-exclusivity CHECK (`NOT (revoked_at AND claimed_at)`) would encode claim semantics L.A1.14 hasn't defined — the review's own probe B shape (claimed_at set AND later revoked_at set) is plausibly *legitimate* for a multi-use invite: some uses claimed, then the commissioner revokes further use; a CHECK baked in now risks a revert migration the day the claim RPC defines this. **(b)** `CHECK (use_count <= max_uses)` alone is safer but settles a clamp-vs-block question that belongs to the RPC (055's own comment says commissioners "may raise" `max_uses` — whether one may ever *lower* it below `use_count` is undefined), and shipping half the backstop invites false confidence. **(c)** Cost: a CHECK-only migration would take 057 and shift the schema lane a third time in one week (057 def_ya → 058, cascading the §7 table + PROGRESS §2 again) for two backstops on a table with **zero writers of any kind** — failing the D53 proportionality test 056's status CHECK passed (that one was free, in-table, at creation). The invariants land as `claim_league_invite`'s in-body duty with pins, tracked as F6 so L.A1.14 can't start without tripping over it |

### Should-fix

- **R47 · should-fix · no consolidated forward-obligations ledger (PROGRESS-leagues.md).** The pre-auth claim preview's field-exposure boundary (§12.23 as amended by D48: league name + team label + inviter display name — explicitly **not** `invited_email`) has no pgTAP pin yet, because `get_join_preview` (L.A1.14) doesn't exist. That's expected — but unlike D53's explicit phrasing for `retire_franchise`'s cycle-rejection duty ("a pgTAP pin due when that RPC lands (M4, D42)", `PROGRESS:212`), no comparable "pin due" sentence exists anywhere for this one; `055_league_invites.sql:32-36` and D48 (`PROGRESS:199`) describe the *plan*, not a tracked duty a future session is instructed to check off. Auditing PROGRESS for siblings turned up at least two more of the same shape, none consolidated: (a) R46's "started_at-reuse hand-off note" (`PROGRESS:384,403`) — the claim/assign RPCs (L.A1.14/L.A1.15) "must close-before-open and never reuse a `started_at`" or collide with `team_managers`' `UNIQUE(team_id, user_id, started_at)`; (b) D55's deferred `league_weeks` status-*transition* validation (`PROGRESS:218`) — legal transitions between the four CHECK-enumerated values are unenforced until M4's `league-week-advance` job lands, with no forward pin recorded beyond the migration-comment aside. All three (plus D53's own) currently live only inside decisions-log prose or migration-comment asides — nothing a session landing L.A1.14 or M4's schedule engine is pointed at systematically. Recommend a dedicated "Forward obligations" section in PROGRESS (or a checklist column) enumerating at minimum: `get_join_preview` field-exposure pin (L.A1.14), `retire_franchise` cycle-rejection pin (M4/D42, already named in D53), claim/assign `started_at`-reuse guard (L.A1.14/L.A1.15), `league_weeks` status-transition trigger (M4 `league-week-advance`).

### Nit

- **R48 · nit · `league_invites` has no algebraic CHECK backstops (055_league_invites.sql:57-73) — probed live (local stack, rolled back).** None of the following are constrained at the DB layer: `use_count` exceeding `max_uses` (inserted `max_uses=1, use_count=5` cleanly); `revoked_at` and `claimed_by`/`claimed_at` set simultaneously (inserted cleanly — a row can be both "claimed" and "revoked" with no ordering relationship enforced, confirmed both `claimed→revoked` and the reverse timestamp order both insert cleanly); `expires_at` in the past at INSERT time (inserted cleanly, `already_expired = true`). All of this is currently inert — no role has a write path to the table at all — so the gap is `claim_league_invite`'s (L.A1.14) future in-body responsibility, the same "no scheduled owner yet" situation R40/D53 already named for the succession-cycle gap. Unlike that gap, this one isn't recorded as a hand-off anywhere, and — notably — 056 shipped in the very same session added exactly this class of free algebraic backstop pre-emptively (the `status` CHECK, justified specifically because "the same gap on a fresh table gets fixed at creation instead of waiting for a review pass," `056_league_weeks.sql:19-21`) while 055 didn't extend that reasoning to its own table's obvious candidates (`CHECK (use_count <= max_uses)`, `CHECK (NOT (revoked_at IS NOT NULL AND claimed_at IS NOT NULL))`). Cheap, no rush given the zero-writer state, but worth a line in R47's ledger so it isn't the thing nobody remembers when L.A1.14 starts writing to this table.

The helper should emit the full family as explicit 0/1 whenever the raw source key is delivered (or the calculator must treat a delivered raw source as delivering its derived family). Neither D44, D56(3), nor the L.A1.8 task text records which; L.A1.8's boundary tests as specced would not catch it (they test bucket edges, not badge semantics).

## Review findings — 2026-07-21 (M1 batch 5)

*Reviewer session (fresh context, red-team brief): diff `da0a4f3..HEAD` (PR #37) — L.A1.8 (§7.3.3 generic dot-product calculator + D44 tier-indicator derivation, 60 vitest; F20 discharge) — verified against spec v2.8.3 §7.3.3/§23.5/E38/E61, D33/D44/D57, and the §6 ledger. Method: **semantics review, not schema** — the pending/zero boundary, the F20 structural guarantee, and the rounding contract each probed with live constructed inputs (`tsx` against the shipped modules); **the NUMERIC(8,2) rounding claim tested live on the local stack** (temp table on `supabase_db_fieldscout`, both numeric-literal and float8 assignment paths), not reasoned from documentation; full 60-test suite re-run green this session. Review-only — nothing fixed. Findings R57–R61 (4 should-fix, 1 nit); **no blockers**.*

**Verified sound (for the record):**

- **The §7.3.3 dot-product contract holds under adversarial inputs.** Unknown stat keys ignored (probed: extra keys leave the breakdown deep-equal); undelivered rules keys land in `pending` with no `perKey` entry in every path — a stat delivered AS `null` (out-of-type but JSONB-realistic) resolves pending via the `typeof`/`isFinite` gate, same as absent, which is the honest reading; delivered-zero is the pinned opposite (real `perKey: 0`, no badge). No mid-computation delivery hazard exists: `Object.entries(rules)` snapshots and the function is pure/synchronous.
- **F20's structural guarantee survives every input shape tried.** Inbound `def_pa_*`/`def_ya_*` keys are stripped unconditionally and re-emitted only from a delivered source (probed: bogus `def_pa_46_plus: 1` without source → absent; with source → recomputed to 0); families deliver independently (PA=0 without YA → full PA family 0/1, all 9 YA keys absent → exactly the YA rules keys pending — tested and re-probed); non-finite source → family absent → pending. Within `deriveTierIndicators` an indicator-without-source state is unconstructible. *(The guarantee is module-local — the composition obligation is R60(b).)*
- **NUMERIC(8,2) live result: half away from zero, matching the implementation.** Temp-table assignment on the local stack: `−0.005 → −0.01`, `−0.015 → −0.02`, `−2.675 → −2.68`, float8 path identical — `roundHalfUp`'s pinned `−0.005 → −0.01` is exactly what the DB does. D57(2)'s reading is now live-verified, not just asserted. *(The spec-term ambiguity is R57.)*
- **E38's round(sum)≠sum(round) rule is genuinely carried by the property test.** The 250-run recompute accumulates at full precision then rounds once; generated contributions carry 3 decimals, so a Σ-of-rounded-perKey implementation diverges from the recompute with near-certainty across 250×~12 keys. `perKey` full-precision values cannot currently leak into a display path: the modules have **zero consumers** (grep-verified — only the two modules + their tests), and both the interface docstring and D57(3) mark `perKey` as full-precision/not-the-stored-total. *(That no stored-literal pin discriminates the two implementations is R61.)*
- **The one-hot property would catch both bug classes asked of it, on the integer domain.** A both-buckets-hot bug fails the per-platform sums (=2) at every overlapping value; an off-by-one boundary (e.g. `def_pa_14_17` hi=18 or hi=16) fails the sum at the seam integer (=2 or =0) inside the scanned PA 0–80 / YA −30–650 ranges, independently of the edge pins — the pins and the property each catch it alone. The platform-set literals in the test are themselves anchored by the both-directions registry cross-check. *(The property's integer-only domain is the gap — R58.)*
- **Record integrity clean.** D57 matches what shipped item-for-item (test count 60 re-run green; 11/9 family sizes; −0.005 pin; toPrecision(13) snap; mulberry32 seeding; key-agnostic calculator verified — no registry import); the prompt-vs-doc discrepancy (the task prompt's "migration 058" parenthetical vs tasks-M1's pure-TS L.A1.8) is recorded in both D57 and the PR #37 body; F20's flip carries the required semantics + pointer (D57(1)) and correctly records "no ruled alternative"; branch+PR discipline respected (`feat/M1-L.A1.8-scoring-calculator`, PR #37); session-log claims re-verified.

### Resolution — 2026-07-21 (Builder, branch `fix/M1-batch5-review-findings-R57-R61`)

*All four should-fixes resolved; nit R61 taken. Semantics decisions recorded as **D58** (both R58 and R59 required a deliberate ruling, not just a guard). Proof (shown in session): fresh `db reset` 001–057 clean · `test:db` **435/435** · vitest **234/234** (12 new in `src/lib/leagues/scoring/`) · `test:gate` **24/24** · type-check clean · lint (pre-existing `auction-draft-room.tsx` warning only). **Three new-pin break probes run live then reverted:** (i) R58 guard removed (emit family whenever the source is finite — the exact pre-fix behavior) → **exactly the 6 new R58 tests fail** (the 4 gap-value pins at 13.5/−3/14.5/99.5, the family-independence test, the unconstructibility counter-pin), nothing else; (ii) R59 throw removed → **exactly the 5 new corrupt-coefficient pins fail**, nothing else; (iii) Σ-of-rounded-perKey substituted for full-precision accumulation → the R61 literal pin fails (0.66 ≠ 0.67) alongside the 250-run property, confirming the pin discriminates on its own.*

| Findings | Resolved by |
|---|---|
| R57 | **Spec erratum v2.8.4** — §7.3.3 precision rule gains the parenthetical: "half-up" = round half **away from zero**, matching `NUMERIC(8,2)` assignment (`−0.005 → −0.01`, citing the review's live temp-table evidence and D57(2)); changelog entry names both ambiguous readings and why E38's determinism rule forces the DB-matching one. Header Version field corrected 2.8.1 → 2.8.4 in passing (stale since v2.8.2). Fold-back rule satisfied — the resolution lives in the LAW, not only the decisions log |
| R58 | this commit — **unmappable source → family PENDING (option b), ruled and recorded as D58(1)**: the published buckets are **integer-gapped, not real-contiguous** (ESPN prints `1–6 / 7–13 / 14–17…` — real-valued gaps at 13.5/99.5 between adjacent buckets), so there is no defensible clamp target and the domain is the integers. `familyMaps` (derive-stats.ts) requires integer-in-≥1-bucket; fractional (even in-interval: 14.5), negative-PA, and non-finite sources all withhold the family → honest pending. Pinned at the review's exact gap values (13.5 / 99.5 / negative PA); one-hot property extended to fractional/negative/non-finite sweeps (*delivered ⇔ integer-in-a-bucket; delivered ⇒ one-hot per platform set*); named counter-pin proves the all-zero-family state is unconstructible. Break probe (i) proves the pins catch the pre-fix behavior |
| R59 | this commit — **corrupt coefficient THROWS, ruled and recorded as D58(2)**: up-front validation over ALL rules keys (typeof number + finite); TypeError names every offending key; string-numerics throw (coercion masks corruption); corrupt keys with no delivered stat still throw (never quietly parked as pending — the reviewer's misbadge concern). Pinned: NaN can never reach the return value (adversarial-shape pin: throw-or-finite-total; finite-total assertion added inside the 250-run property). Worker-side catch posture explicitly handed to L.D1 via F23. Break probe (ii) proves the pins catch the unguarded implementation |
| R60 | this commit — **F22** (writer-path rounding pin at the L.D1 worker, with the live DB evidence citation) and **F23** (derive→score composition pinned at `score-league-week`; L.A1.10 = interim proof-of-pattern, not discharge; + the D58(2) throw-posture decision routed there) added to §6, both → discharged by L.D1 (M4) |
| R61 (nit, taken) | this commit — stored-literal discriminating pin added: `{a: 0.111, b: 0.111} × {a: 3, b: 3} → total 0.67` (a Σ-of-rounded-perKey implementation yields 0.66); §4.3(a)'s falsifiable-literal bar met beside the statistical property |

### Should-fix

- **R57 · should-fix · calculator.ts:36-49 (`roundHalfUp` doc) + spec §7.3.3 precision rule — "half-up" is ecosystem-ambiguous and the spec never pins which meaning; the implementation's choice is right but lives only in the decisions log; refs: E38 (determinism), D57(2), the §3 fold-back discipline.** The two authorities the rounding question names genuinely diverge at negative half-cent boundaries: strict "round half up" (the Wikipedia/IEEE toward-+∞ convention) gives `−0.005 → −0.00`, while Postgres `NUMERIC(8,2)` assignment — **tested live this session, both numeric-literal and float8 paths** — gives `−0.005 → −0.01` (half away from zero; Java's `RoundingMode.HALF_UP` also means away-from-zero, so the bare term supports both readings). The implementation sides with the DB, which is the only coherent choice under E38's determinism rule (JS result ≡ stored result), and pins it at `−0.005 → −0.01`. But the spec text still says bare "half-up": a future implementer (or the L.D1 writer's author) reading §7.3.3 alone can pick the toward-+∞ reading and diverge from both this module and the column type on negative totals (all-miss K weeks, INT-heavy QB weeks). The divergence window is narrow — with 2-decimal template coefficients × integer stats every sum is exact at 2 decimals, so a negative half-cent boundary needs ≥3-decimal coefficients — but the determinism rule is absolute and the fix is one line. Fold a spec erratum (changelog + §7.3.3 parenthetical): *"half-up = round half away from zero, matching NUMERIC(8,2) assignment; −0.005 → −0.01"* — per the repo's own rule that resolved ambiguities get folded back into the spec, not left in D-rows.
- **R58 · should-fix · derive-stats.ts:83-91 (`emitFamily`) — a delivered source value that lands in no bucket emits an all-zero family: delivered-zero everywhere, nothing hot, nothing pending — the silent-wrong-total class E61/F20 exist to prevent; refs: §23.5, E61, F20, D57(1).** Probed live: `def_points_allowed: 13.5` → all 11 PA keys delivered as 0 (13.5 falls between `def_pa_7_13` and `def_pa_14_20`; both platform sums = 0, violating the one-hot invariant the property test asserts — but that property only scans **integers**, so the state is invisible to it); `def_yards_allowed: 99.5` → same for YA; a (bogus) negative PA → same, since `def_pa_0` pins lo=0. Downstream, every tier rules key scores a delivered 0 with an empty `pending` — for ESPN templates that silently erases the D/ST tier component (up to ±5 points/side) with no badge, which is a *worse* state than the pre-F20 all-pending bug (that one was at least honest). Reachability today is low — `player_stats` sources are INTEGER columns (057), and nothing calls these modules yet — but the helper's input is provider-shaped `Record<string, number>`, not column-shaped, and nothing range-guards it. Cheap close: treat a source value matching **no** bucket as unreported (family absent → pending; for PA that also covers negatives), and extend the one-hot property to assert *delivered ⇒ exactly one hot per platform set* over non-integer samples so the wrong fix (leaving the gap) can't pass.
- **R59 · should-fix · calculator.ts:64-72 — the delivered-stat guard is one-sided: a malformed rules **coefficient** poisons the total to NaN, and NUMERIC(8,2) accepts NaN, so the poisoned total is storable; refs: E61 ("never a silent wrong total"), §7.3.3 snapshot rule, D57(3)'s defensive clause.** Probed live: `{ receptions: "abc" }` (rules values come from `scoring_rules_snapshot` JSONB, which can carry strings) → `contribution = NaN` → `total: NaN` (`roundHalfUp` propagates it), and `INSERT 'NaN'` into a `NUMERIC(8,2)` column **succeeds** (live-tested — Postgres numeric has a NaN value), so nothing downstream is guaranteed to throw. A string-numeric coefficient (`"0.5"`) silently coerces and scores (×4 → 2) — works by accident, masking a corrupt snapshot. The module already defends the *stats* side against exactly this class (non-finite → pending, D57's "never poisoning a total"); the rules side, same trust boundary (a JSONB blob), has no guard. Reachability requires a corrupt snapshot — v1 rules are code-authored templates frozen via the L.A1.11 RPC — but the v1.1 custom editor (F21's scope) makes rules user-shaped, and this module is the last line. Semantics call for the Builder to make **and record**: marking a bad-coefficient key `pending` would misbadge corrupt config as "waiting on data," so the honest options are throw (corrupt snapshot = programming/config error, loud) or a distinct breakdown flag; either way, pin it with a test.
- **R60 · should-fix · PROGRESS §6 — two hand-offs this task created live only in prose; per the §6 header rule (R51: "creating a hand-off without an F-row is itself a review finding") both need rows; refs: R51 precedent, E38, F20/D57(1), D44.** **(a) Rounding vs the real writer (L.D1):** D57(2)'s NUMERIC-matching claim carried no DB evidence at build time (this review supplied it — live temp-table proof, see header), and the actual consistency obligation — the L.D1 writer stores the **JS-rounded** total into `matchups`/`team_week_results` `NUMERIC(8,2)`, with a pin proving no path lets the DB do the rounding on a diverging value (benign only while both round half-away-from-zero; E38's determinism must be *pinned* at the writer, not inherited silently) — belongs to a task that doesn't exist yet in any ledger row. Add F22 → discharged by L.D1 (M4). **(b) The derive→score composition:** F20's guarantee is module-local — `scorePlayerWeek` happily accepts raw stats that never passed `deriveTierIndicators` (tier rules keys then read pending forever), and a worker that skips derive also loses the inbound-indicator stripping (D44's "never stored" laundering guard). The composition appears only in L.A1.10's task-text usage sketch (`scorePlayerWeek(template.rules, derive(stats))`); the production call site is M4's `score-league-week` (§22.2/L.D1), and no F-row keys it. Add F23 → *`score-league-week` composes `deriveTierIndicators` before `scorePlayerWeek`; pinned at the worker* — discharged by L.D1 (M4), with L.A1.10 as the interim proof-of-pattern.

### Nit

- **R61 · nit · calculator.test.ts — no stored-literal golden pin discriminates round(full sum) from Σ(rounded perKey); only the 250-run property carries E38's precision rule; refs: §4.3(a) (golden pins as stored literals), E38, D57(3).** Every literal pin's contributions are exact at ≤2 decimals (QB 11.48+8−2+2+1.2; E38 tie 9.2+6+0.04) — a Σ-of-rounded implementation passes all of them; the single-key `0.335×3 → 1.01` pin rounds identically under both (one entry). The property test does catch the wrong implementation (near-certainly, per the verified-sound note), but §4.3(a)'s point is a falsifiable *literal*: one two-key pin, e.g. rules `{a: 0.111, b: 0.111}` × stats `{a: 3, b: 3}` → full sum 0.666 → `total: 0.67` (a Σ-of-rounded impl yields 0.33+0.33 = **0.66**), pins the rule as a stored literal instead of a statistical consequence.
